### FILE: market_data\builder.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/builder.py
from datetime import date
import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_defs import (
    interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d,
)
from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
from riskquant.market_data.surfaces.y_axes_types import (
    SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.compiled import CompiledPortfolio
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_dispatch import CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY
from riskquant.utils.dates import date_to_ordinal32


def _build_curve_grid(
    raw_tenors: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (C, M)
    raw_values: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (C, M, S)
    target_dates: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (D,)
    interp_methods: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (C,) int8
    extrap_methods: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (C,) int8
) -> np.ndarray:
    C, M, S = raw_values.shape
    D = len(target_dates)
    out_grid = np.empty((C, D, S), dtype=np.float64)
    if D == 0:
        return out_grid

    for (interp_id, extrap_id), math_kernel in CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY.items():
        mask = (interp_methods == interp_id) & (extrap_methods == extrap_id)
        if np.any(mask):
            out_grid[mask] = math_kernel(
                raw_tenors[mask], raw_values[mask], target_dates
            )
    return out_grid


def build_market_data_matrix(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    portfolio: CompiledPortfolio,
    num_scenarios: int,
    # Scalars
    raw_fx_spots: np.ndarray,
    raw_prices: np.ndarray,
    raw_forward_nodes: np.ndarray,
    raw_vol_nodes: np.ndarray,
    # 1D Configs
    curve_interp_methods: np.ndarray,
    curve_extrap_methods: np.ndarray,
    raw_discount_tenors: np.ndarray,
    raw_discount_ln_dfs: np.ndarray,
    raw_price_curve_tenors: np.ndarray,
    raw_price_curve_values: np.ndarray,
    # 2D Configs & Surfaces
    surface_interp_x_methods: np.ndarray,  # Time interpolation config
    surface_extrap_x_methods: np.ndarray,  # Time extrapolation config
    surface_y_axis_type_ids: np.ndarray,  # Y-Axis Config
    raw_vol_surface_tenors: np.ndarray,  # (C_surf, M_tenors)
    raw_vol_surface_strikes: np.ndarray,  # (C_surf, K_strikes)
    raw_vol_surface_values: np.ndarray,  # (C_surf, M_tenors, K_strikes, S_scenarios)
) -> MarketDataMatrix:

    D = len(portfolio.unique_dates_ordinal32)

    # 1. DISCOUNT GRID
    if D == 0:
        discount_grid = np.empty(
            (raw_discount_ln_dfs.shape[0], 0, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64
        )
    else:
        discount_grid = interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d(
            tenors=raw_discount_tenors,
            values=raw_discount_ln_dfs,
            targets=portfolio.unique_dates_ordinal32,
        )

    # 2. PRICE GRID
    price_curve_grid = _build_curve_grid(
        raw_tenors=raw_price_curve_tenors,
        raw_values=raw_price_curve_values,
        target_dates=portfolio.unique_dates_ordinal32,
        interp_methods=curve_interp_methods,
        extrap_methods=curve_extrap_methods,
    )

    # 3. TTE GRID
    as_of_ord = date_to_ordinal32(as_of_date)
    tte_grid = np.maximum(
        (portfolio.unique_dates_ordinal32 - as_of_ord) / 365.25, 1e-9
    )

    # 4. VOLATILITY SURFACES (Phase 1 Time-Slicing via Reshape Trick)
    if np.any(surface_y_axis_type_ids == SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL):
        raise ValueError(
            "FATAL: Delta-quoted Volatility Surfaces detected in runtime builder. "
            "DELTA_CALL surfaces must be pre-processed and converted to STRIKE or MONEYNESS "
            "by the Calibration module before being passed to the MarketDataMatrix."
        )

    if D == 0 or raw_vol_surface_values.size == 0:
        C_surf = (
            raw_vol_surface_tenors.shape[0]
            if raw_vol_surface_tenors.ndim > 0
            else 0
        )
        K_strikes = (
            raw_vol_surface_strikes.shape[1]
            if raw_vol_surface_strikes.ndim > 1
            else 0
        )
        vol_surfaces = np.empty(
            (C_surf, 0, K_strikes, num_scenarios),
            dtype=np.float64,
        )
    else:
        C_surf, M_tenors, K_strikes, S_scen = raw_vol_surface_values.shape

        # Flatten Strike & Scenarios
        flat_surfaces = raw_vol_surface_values.reshape(
            C_surf,
            M_tenors,
            K_strikes * S_scen,
        )

        time_sliced_smiles_flat = _build_curve_grid(
            raw_tenors=raw_vol_surface_tenors,
            raw_values=flat_surfaces,
            target_dates=portfolio.unique_dates_ordinal32,
            interp_methods=surface_interp_x_methods,
            extrap_methods=surface_extrap_x_methods,
        )

        # Reshape back to 4D
        vol_surfaces = time_sliced_smiles_flat.reshape(
            C_surf,
            D,
            K_strikes,
            S_scen,
        )

    return MarketDataMatrix(
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
        fx_spots=raw_fx_spots,
        prices=raw_prices,
        forward_nodes=raw_forward_nodes,
        vol_nodes=raw_vol_nodes,
        discount_factor_grid=discount_grid,
        price_curve_grid=price_curve_grid,
        tte_grid=tte_grid,
        vol_surface_strikes=raw_vol_surface_strikes,
        vol_surfaces=vol_surfaces,
    )

```

### FILE: market_data\curves\extrap_methods.py ###

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\curves\extrap_methods.py

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class ExtrapMethod(IntEnum):
    FLAT = 1
    LINEAR = 2


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
EXTRAP_FLAT: np.int8 = np.int8(ExtrapMethod.FLAT)
EXTRAP_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(ExtrapMethod.LINEAR)


# Extrap methods
EXTRAP_METHOD_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in ExtrapMethod
)

# Central registry
EXTRAP_METHOD_REGISTRY: dict[str, ExtrapMethod] = {
    "LINEAR": ExtrapMethod.LINEAR,
    "FLAT": ExtrapMethod.FLAT,
}


def validate_extrapolation_methods_dict(
    extrapolation_methods_dict: dict[str, int],
) -> None:
    expected = {
        name: int(member) for name, member in ExtrapMethod.__members__.items()
    }
    got = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in extrapolation_methods_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "extrapolation_methods sheet does not match riskquant ExtrapMethod enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel extrapolation_methods table or update riskquant.market_data.curves.extrap_methods.ExtrapMethod."
    )

```

### FILE: market_data\curves\interp_defs.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/curves/interp_defs.py
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

# --- LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---


def interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Linear Interpolation with Flat Extrapolation.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx1 = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2),
        1,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    t0, t1 = tenors[c_idx, idx0], tenors[c_idx, idx1]
    y0, y1 = values[c_idx, idx0, :], values[c_idx, idx1, :]

    dt = np.where((t1 - t0) == 0, 1.0, t1 - t0)
    w = (targets[None, :] - t0) / dt

    # FLAT EXTRAPOLATION: Clamp weights to [0.0, 1.0]
    w = np.clip(w, 0.0, 1.0)

    return y0 + w[:, :, None] * (y1 - y0)


# --- LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---


def interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Linear Interpolation with Linear Extrapolation.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx1 = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2),
        1,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    t0, t1 = tenors[c_idx, idx0], tenors[c_idx, idx1]
    y0, y1 = values[c_idx, idx0, :], values[c_idx, idx1, :]

    dt = np.where((t1 - t0) == 0, 1.0, t1 - t0)
    w = (targets[None, :] - t0) / dt

    # LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION: Do not clamp weights
    return y0 + w[:, :, None] * (y1 - y0)


# --- LOG-LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION (ALWAYS USED FOR DISCOUNT FACTORS) ---

# RQ always interps on discount factor curves using log-linear interpolation with flat extrapolation.


def interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Log-Linear Interpolation with Constant Forward Extrapolation.

    Mathematically equivalent to linear interpolation on Zero Rates.
    To note: values are expected to be in natural log space.
    By allowing the interpolation weight `w` to exceed 1.0 for targets past the
    final tenor, we linearly extrapolate the log discount factor, which perfectly
    preserves the forward rate of the final pillar segment.

    Shapes:
        tenors:     (C_curves, M_pillars)
        values:     (C_curves, M_pillars, S_scenarios)
        targets:    (D_unique_dates,)

    Returns:
        np.ndarray: Exponentiated Discount Factors. Shape (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_targets = len(targets)

    # 1. VECTORIZED BOUNDARY SEARCH
    # Expand dims to broadcast: (c_curves, 1, m_pillars) <= (1, d_targets, 1) -> (c_curves, d_targets, m_pillars)
    # Summing across the pillars (axis=2) gives the index of the first tenor > the target.
    mask = tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None]
    idx1 = np.sum(mask, axis=2)

    # Clip to safely anchor both interpolation and extrapolation to the known bounds.
    idx1 = np.clip(idx1, 1, m_pillars - 1)
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    # 2. ADVANCED INTEGER INDEXING
    # Extract bounding tenors and values instantly using a broadcasted column vector
    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]

    t0 = tenors[c_idx, idx0]  # Shape: (c_curves, d_targets)
    t1 = tenors[c_idx, idx1]  # Shape: (c_curves, d_targets)

    y0 = values[c_idx, idx0, :]  # Shape: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    y1 = values[c_idx, idx1, :]  # Shape: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)

    # 3. CALCULATE WEIGHTS
    dt = t1 - t0
    dt = np.where(dt == 0, 1.0, dt)  # Prevent division-by-zero on sentinels

    # Shape: (c_curves, d_targets)
    w = (targets[None, :] - t0) / dt

    # Note: Deliberately NO np.clip(w, 0.0, 1.0) here.
    # Allowing w > 1.0 natively creates Constant Forward Extrapolation.

    # 4. LOG-LINEAR MATH & EXPONENTIATION
    # Expand weights to (c_curves, d_targets, 1) to broadcast against the Scenarios dimension (s_scenarios)
    ln_interp = y0 + w[:, :, None] * (y1 - y0)

    return np.exp(ln_interp)


# --- LOG-LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---


def interp_log_linear_linear_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Log-Linear Interpolation with Linear Extrapolation.
    To note: values are expected to be in natural log space.
    Extrapolates using linear forward rate.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios) - Exponentiated back to raw values
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx1 = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2),
        1,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    t0, t1 = tenors[c_idx, idx0], tenors[c_idx, idx1]
    y0, y1 = values[c_idx, idx0, :], values[c_idx, idx1, :]

    dt = np.where((t1 - t0) == 0, 1.0, t1 - t0)
    w = (targets[None, :] - t0) / dt

    # LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION: Do not clamp weights
    ln_interp = y0 + w[:, :, None] * (y1 - y0)
    return np.exp(ln_interp)


# --- STEP LEFT (LOCF) INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---


def interp_step_left_flat_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Step Left (LOCF) Interpolation.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2) - 1,
        0,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]

    return values[c_idx, idx, :]


# --- STEP LEFT (LOCF) INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---


def interp_step_left_linear_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Step Left (LOCF) Interpolation with Linear Extrapolation.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2) - 1,
        0,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]

    # For linear extrapolation, we can simply use the last two points to define a slope for extrapolation.
    # However, since this is a step function, the "slope" is zero. Thus, step left with linear extrapolation is effectively the same as step left with flat extrapolation.

    return values[c_idx, idx, :]


# --- STEP RIGHT (NOCB) INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---


def interp_step_right_flat_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Step Right (NOCB) Interpolation.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] < targets[None, :, None], axis=2),
        0,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]

    return values[c_idx, idx, :]


# --- STEP RIGHT (NOCB) INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---


def interp_step_right_linear_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Step Right (NOCB) Interpolation with Linear Extrapolation.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] < targets[None, :, None], axis=2),
        0,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]

    # For linear extrapolation, we can simply use the last two points to define a slope for extrapolation.
    # However, since this is a step function, the "slope" is zero. Thus, step right with linear extrapolation is effectively the same as step right with flat extrapolation.

    return values[c_idx, idx, :]


# --- 2D SURFACE STRIKE INTERPOLATOR ---


def interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap(
    pillars: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (N_trades, K_strikes)
    values: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (N_trades, K_strikes, S_scenarios)
    targets: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (N_trades, S_scenarios)
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 1D linear interpolation along the strike/Y-axis with flat extrapolation.
    Used dynamically inside Option Pricers to resolve exact strike vol across scenarios.
    """
    N, K_dim = pillars.shape
    S = targets.shape[1]

    # Broadcast pillars to (N, S, K) to compare against targets (N, S, 1)
    mask = pillars[:, None, :] <= targets[:, :, None]
    idx1 = np.sum(mask, axis=2)
    idx1 = np.clip(idx1, 1, K_dim - 1)
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    n_idx = np.arange(N)[:, None]
    s_idx = np.arange(S)[None, :]

    # Extract bounding pillars
    x0 = pillars[n_idx, idx0]  # (N, S)
    x1 = pillars[n_idx, idx1]  # (N, S)

    # Extract bounding values
    y0 = values[n_idx, idx0, s_idx]  # (N, S)
    y1 = values[n_idx, idx1, s_idx]  # (N, S)

    dx = np.where((x1 - x0) == 0, 1.0, x1 - x0)
    w = (targets - x0) / dx

    # Flat Extrapolation
    w = np.clip(w, 0.0, 1.0)

    return y0 + w * (y1 - y0)

```

```python
    interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d,

    interp_step_left_linear_extrap_3d,
    interp_step_right_flat_extrap_3d,
    interp_step_right_linear_extrap_3d,
)

# ... [All your pure math functions here] ...

# ============================================================
# INTERPOLATION DISPATCH REGISTRY
# ============================================================

# Define the strict signature every curve kernel must follow
InterpKernelFn = Callable[[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray], np.ndarray]

# Map (Interp Method ID, Extrap Method ID) -> Math Kernel
# We cast the np.int8 enums to pure Python ints for safe, fast dictionary hashing.
CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY: dict[tuple[int, int], InterpKernelFn] = {
    (
        int(INTERP_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d,
    (
        int(INTERP_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d,
    (
        int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d,
    (
        int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_log_linear_linear_extrap_3d,
    (
        int(INTERP_STEP_LEFT),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_step_left_flat_extrap_3d,
    (
        int(INTERP_STEP_LEFT),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_step_left_linear_extrap_3d,
    (
        int(INTERP_STEP_RIGHT),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_step_right_flat_extrap_3d,
    (
        int(INTERP_STEP_RIGHT),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_step_right_linear_extrap_3d,
```

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\curves\interp_methods.py

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum
from typing import Mapping

import numpy as np


class InterpMethod(IntEnum):
    LINEAR = 1
    LOG_LINEAR = 2
    STEP_LEFT = 3
    STEP_RIGHT = 4
    CUBIC_SPLINE = 5


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
INTERP_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.LINEAR)
INTERP_LOG_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.LOG_LINEAR)
INTERP_STEP_LEFT: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.STEP_LEFT)
INTERP_STEP_RIGHT: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.STEP_RIGHT)
INTERP_CUBIC_SPLINE: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.CUBIC_SPLINE)

# Interp methods
INTERP_METHOD_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in InterpMethod
)

# Central registry
INTERP_METHOD_REGISTRY: dict[str, InterpMethod] = {
    "LINEAR": InterpMethod.LINEAR,
    "LOG_LINEAR": InterpMethod.LOG_LINEAR,
    "STEP_LEFT": InterpMethod.STEP_LEFT,
    "STEP_RIGHT": InterpMethod.STEP_RIGHT,
    "CUBIC_SPLINE": InterpMethod.CUBIC_SPLINE,
}


def validate_interpolation_methods_dict(
    interpolation_methods_dict: Mapping[str, int],
) -> None:
    expected = {
        name: int(member) for name, member in InterpMethod.__members__.items()
    }
    got = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in interpolation_methods_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "interpolation_methods sheet does not match riskquant InterpolationMethod enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel interpolation_methods table or update riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_methods.InterpMethod."
    )

```

### FILE: market_data\risk_factors\builder_matrix.py ###
```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\builder_matrix.py
from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path
from typing import Mapping, Optional, Union

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas.api.types import is_datetime64_any_dtype

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.builder_utils import (
    _require_mapping_key,
    _validate_mapping_dict,
)
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.matrix import RiskFactorMatrix
from riskquant.utils.dates import (
    coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp,
    datetime64D_to_ordinal32,
)

# --- BUILD A RISK FACTOR MATRIX FROM A DATAFRAME


def build_risk_factor_matrix_from_df(
    *,
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    name_to_id_map: Optional[Mapping[str, int]] = None,
    use_int32_dates: bool = True,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
    """Construct a RiskFactorMatrix from a wide pandas DataFrame.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : pd.DataFrame
        Wide DataFrame with dates on the index and one column per risk factor.
    name_to_id_map : Optional[Mapping[str, int]]
        Optional mapping from column name (string) to integer factor id. If
        omitted, columns are parsed as integers directly.
    use_int32_dates : bool
        If True convert dates to int32 ordinal representation; otherwise
        keep datetime64[D] dtype.
    float_dtype : numpy floating type
        dtype used for the `values` array (default: np.float32).

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorMatrix
        Canonicalized matrix with validated shapes, dtypes and contiguity.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If the index is not date-like, not monotonic increasing, or contains
        duplicates; or if factor id mapping contains errors.

    Notes
    -----
    The function intentionally does not auto-sort the DataFrame rows
    (dates). If the provided index is unsorted we raise an error because
    auto-sorting would require an expensive O(T*N) copy of the values.
    """
    if not isinstance(df, pd.DataFrame):
        raise ValueError("Expected a DataFrame!")

    # Accept a wider set of date-like index types; coerce if needed.
    if not is_datetime64_any_dtype(df.index):
        # Convert in-place where possible; this preserves row order and
        # avoids unnecessary copying of the data matrix.
        df = df.copy(deep=False)
        df.index = pd.to_datetime(df.index, errors="raise")

    # Enforce invariants early with clear errors (and avoid needless allocations)
    if df.index.has_duplicates:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame index (dates) must be unique!")
    if not df.index.is_monotonic_increasing:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame index (dates) must be sorted ascending!")

    # Check for duplicated column labels (factor names); this would cause silent data corruption
    if df.columns.has_duplicates:
        duplicated = df.columns[df.columns.duplicated(keep=False)]
        unique_dupes = duplicated.unique()
        sample = unique_dupes[:10]
        raise ValueError(
            f"DataFrame columns (factor names) must be unique.\n"
            f"Found {len(unique_dupes)} duplicated column(s).\n"
            f"Sample duplicates: {list(sample)}"
        )

    # Dates: obtain a datetime64[D] view and optionally convert to int32 ordinal
    dts_64D = df.index.to_numpy(dtype="datetime64[D]", copy=False)
    dates = datetime64D_to_ordinal32(dts_64D) if use_int32_dates else dts_64D

    # Columns -> factor_ids: either direct integer parsing or mapping lookup.
    cols = df.columns
    n = cols.size

    if name_to_id_map is None:
        # Parse column labels as integers; will raise on non-integer labels.
        factor_ids = np.fromiter(
            (int(c) for c in cols),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )
    else:
        # Validate mapping once (cheap for small config dicts) and then map.
        _validate_mapping_dict(name_to_id_map)
        factor_ids = np.fromiter(
            (_require_mapping_key(name_to_id_map, str(c)) for c in cols),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )

    # Canonicalize factor axis BEFORE allocating `values` (avoids extra copies).
    if n > 1 and np.any(factor_ids[1:] < factor_ids[:-1]):
        perm = np.argsort(factor_ids, kind="mergesort")
        factor_ids = factor_ids[perm]

        if np.any(factor_ids[1:] == factor_ids[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique!")

        # Reorder the DataFrame columns to match the canonical factor order.
        df = df.iloc[:, perm]
    else:
        if n > 1 and np.any(factor_ids[1:] == factor_ids[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique!")

    # Materialize the numeric matrix as a C-contiguous array with requested dtype.
    values = np.ascontiguousarray(
        df.to_numpy(dtype=float_dtype, copy=True, na_value=np.nan)
    )

    return RiskFactorMatrix(dates=dates, factor_ids=factor_ids, values=values)


# --- BUILD A RISK FACTOR MATRIX DIRECTLY FROM AN XLSX FILE


def build_risk_factor_matrix_from_xlsx(
    *,
    path: Union[str, Path],
    sheet_name: Union[str, int] = 0,
    name_to_id_map: Optional[Mapping[str, int]] = None,
    use_int32_dates: bool = True,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
    as_of_date: Optional[pd.Timestamp] = None,
) -> "RiskFactorMatrix":
    """Load a RiskFactorMatrix from an Excel workbook/sheet.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : str | Path
        Path to the Excel file.
    sheet_name : str | int
        Sheet name or index to read (forwarded to pandas.read_excel).
    name_to_id_map : Optional[Mapping[str, int]]
        Optional mapping for column name -> factor id (same semantics as
        `from_df`).
    use_int32_dates : bool
        If True convert dates to int32 ordinal representation.
    float_dtype : numpy floating type
        dtype used for the `values` array.
    as_of_date : Optional[pd.Timestamp]
        If provided, rows with date > as_of_date are filtered out.

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorMatrix
        Canonicalized matrix constructed from the Excel sheet.
    """
    # Read sheet with the first column treated as the index (dates expected).
    df = pd.read_excel(
        str(path),
        sheet_name=sheet_name,
        index_col=0,
    )

    # If empty.
    if df.empty:
        raise ValueError("dataframe is empty - check: {path}")

    # Convert index to datetime if needed (common for Excel imports).
    if not is_datetime64_any_dtype(df.index):
        df.index = pd.to_datetime(df.index, errors="raise")

    # Optional filter by as_of_date; preserves monotonicity if present.
    if as_of_date is not None:
        cutoff = coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp(as_of_date)
        df = df.loc[df.index <= cutoff]

    # Enforce same sorted/unique contract as `from_df`.

    # Duplicated dates.
    if df.index.has_duplicates:
        duplicated = df.index[df.index.duplicated(keep=False)]
        unique_dupes = duplicated.unique()
        sample = unique_dupes[:10]
        raise ValueError(
            f"Excel sheet index (dates) must be unique.\n"
            f"Found {len(unique_dupes)} duplicated date(s).\n"
            f"Sample duplicates: {list(sample)}"
        )

    # Non-monotonic dates
    if not df.index.is_monotonic_increasing:
        raise ValueError("Excel sheet index (dates) must be sorted ascending!")

    # Reuse the robust `from_df` constructor for the remaining work.
    return build_risk_factor_matrix_from_df(
        df=df,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        use_int32_dates=use_int32_dates,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )

```

### FILE: market_data\risk_factors\builder_snapshot.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/builder_snapshot.py
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Mapping, Optional, Union

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.builder_utils import (
    _require_mapping_key,
    _validate_mapping_dict,
)


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_dict(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    levels_dict: Mapping[str, float],
    name_to_id_map: Optional[Mapping[str, int]] = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a dictionary mapping risk factor names to their levels.
    """
    if not levels_dict:
        raise ValueError("levels_dict cannot be empty.")

    n = len(levels_dict)

    if name_to_id_map is None:
        # Assume the keys in the dictionary are already integer IDs formatted as strings.
        ids = np.fromiter(
            (int(k) for k in levels_dict.keys()), dtype=np.int32, count=n
        )
    else:
        # Resolve names to IDs
        _validate_mapping_dict(name_to_id_map)
        ids = np.fromiter(
            (
                _require_mapping_key(name_to_id_map, str(k))
                for k in levels_dict.keys()
            ),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )

    # Extract levels into a contiguous array
    levels = np.fromiter(levels_dict.values(), dtype=float_dtype, count=n)

    return RiskFactorLevelSnapshot(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        factor_ids=ids,
        factor_levels=levels,
    )


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_df(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    factor_col: str = "risk_factor_name",
    level_col: str = "level",
    name_to_id_map: Optional[Mapping[str, int]] = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a vertical pandas DataFrame.
    """
    if df.empty:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame is empty.")

    if factor_col not in df.columns or level_col not in df.columns:
        raise ValueError(
            f"DataFrame must contain columns '{factor_col}' and '{level_col}'."
        )

    # Drop any rows where the level is NaN
    df_clean = df.dropna(subset=[level_col])

    # There must not be duplicate factor names after dropping NaNs, as this would lead to ambiguity in the snapshot.
    if df_clean[factor_col].duplicated().any():
        duplicated_factors = df_clean[factor_col][
            df_clean[factor_col].duplicated()
        ].unique()
        raise ValueError(
            f"Duplicate factor names found in column '{factor_col}' after dropping NaNs: {duplicated_factors}"
        )

    # Extract as a dictionary to reuse the highly-optimized dictionary builder
    # zip is faster than df.set_index().to_dict() or df.itertuples()
    levels_dict = dict(
        zip(df_clean[factor_col].astype(str), df_clean[level_col])
    )

    return build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_dict(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        levels_dict=levels_dict,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_xlsx(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    path: Union[str, Path],
    sheet_name: Union[str, int] = 0,
    factor_col: str = "risk_factor_name",
    level_col: str = "level",
    name_to_id_map: Optional[Mapping[str, int]] = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a two-column Excel sheet.
    """
    df = pd.read_excel(str(path), sheet_name=sheet_name)

    return build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_df(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        df=df,
        factor_col=factor_col,
        level_col=level_col,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )

```

### FILE: market_data\risk_factors\builder_utils.py ###
```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\builder_utils.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Mapping

import numpy as np


def _validate_mapping_dict(m: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Validate a small static mapping of risk factor name -> integer id.

    Checks performed:
      - keys must be non-empty strings
      - values must be integer-like (reject bool, float, str)
      - no two keys map to the same integer id

    This helper is optimized for configuration-time validation (small dicts),
    not for per-row lookups in tight loops.
    """
    # Empty/blank key detection (single pass)
    for k in m.keys():
        if not isinstance(k, str) or not k.strip():
            raise ValueError(f"Mapping contains invalid/blank key: {k!r}")

    # Ensure values are integer-like and detect duplicates by tracking seen ids.
    seen: dict[int, str] = {}
    for k, v in m.items():
        # `bool` is a subclass of int in Python; explicitly reject it here because
        # booleans are not valid factor ids.
        if isinstance(v, (bool,)):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Mapping value for '{k}' must be an int id, got bool={v!r}"
            )

        # Accept Python ints and NumPy integer scalars; reject other types.
        if not isinstance(v, (int, np.integer)):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Mapping value for '{k}' must be an int id, got {type(v).__name__}={v!r}"
            )

        iv = int(v)
        prev = seen.get(iv)
        if prev is not None:
            # Clear, actionable error message when multiple names map to same id.
            raise ValueError(
                "Mapping contains duplicate ID assignment:\n"
                f"  '{prev}' and '{k}' both map to ID={iv}\n"
                "Each risk factor must have a unique risk_factor_name_id."
            )
        seen[iv] = k


def _require_mapping_key(
    m: Mapping[str, Any],
    key: str,
    *,
    preview_n: int = 25,
) -> Any:
    """Lookup `key` in mapping `m` and raise a detailed error if missing.

    This helper provides user-friendly diagnostics on configuration errors by
    showing a short preview of known keys and a suggested fix. Designed to be
    inexpensive on the success path (single hash lookup); the preview list is
    materialized only on error.

    Parameters
    ----------
    m : Mapping[str, Any]
        Mapping from risk factor name to id.
    key : str
        Key to lookup (column label converted to string prior to lookup).
    preview_n : int
        Maximum number of known keys to show in the error message.
    """
    try:
        return m[key]
    except KeyError:
        # Materialize a small preview of keys only when diagnosing a failure --
        # prevents huge error messages for very large config maps.
        keys = list(m.keys())
        n = len(keys)
        preview = keys[:preview_n]
        preview_str = ", ".join(preview) + (" ..." if n > preview_n else "")

        raise ValueError(
            "Risk factor ID mapping lookup failed.\n"
            f"Missing key: '{key}'\n\n"
            "Fix:\n"
            "  Add this risk factor to the STATIC config table that defines risk_factor_name_id.\n"
            "  Example row to add:\n"
            f"    risk_factor_name = {key}\n"
            "    risk_factor_name_id = <new unique integer>\n\n"
            f"Known keys preview ({min(preview_n, n)} of {n}):\n"
            f"  {preview_str}\n"
        ) from None

```

### FILE: market_data\risk_factors\matrix.py ###
```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/matrix.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date
from typing import Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot
from riskquant.utils.dates import ordinal32_to_datetime64D


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class RiskFactorMatrix:
    """
    Canonical RiskFactorMatrix with God-Tier memory layout for time-series operations.

    Invariants:
      - dates: 1D, sorted ascending, unique (int32 ordinal or datetime64[D])
      - factor_ids: 1D int32, sorted ascending, unique
      - values: 2D float32/64, shape (T, N), F-contiguous by default for column-major access
      - len(dates)=T, len(factor_ids)=N
    """

    # datetime64[D] or int32 ordinal
    dates: np.ndarray

    # int32
    factor_ids: np.ndarray

    # float32/64, shape (T, N)
    values: np.ndarray

    # Default memory layout (F-contiguous for time-series)
    fortran_order: bool = True

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        # ---- Basic checks ----
        T, N = self.values.shape
        if self.dates.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError("dates must be 1D")
        if self.factor_ids.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be 1D")
        if self.values.ndim != 2:
            raise ValueError("values must be 2D")

        # ---- dtype checks ----
        if not np.issubdtype(self.values.dtype, np.floating):
            raise TypeError(
                f"values must be float dtype, got {self.values.dtype}"
            )

        if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer):
            if self.dates.dtype != np.int32:
                raise TypeError(
                    f"int dates must be int32, got {self.dates.dtype}"
                )
        elif self.dates.dtype != np.dtype("datetime64[D]"):
            raise TypeError(
                f"datetime dates must be datetime64[D], got {self.dates.dtype}"
            )

        if self.factor_ids.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(
                f"factor_ids must be int32, got {self.factor_ids.dtype}"
            )

        # ---- shape checks ----
        if self.dates.size != T:
            raise ValueError(
                f"len(dates) ({self.dates.size}) != values.shape[0] ({T})"
            )
        if self.factor_ids.size != N:
            raise ValueError(
                f"len(factor_ids) ({self.factor_ids.size}) != values.shape[1] ({N})"
            )

        # ---- memory layout ----
        if self.fortran_order:
            if not self.values.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
                vals = np.asfortranarray(self.values)
                object.__setattr__(self, "values", vals)
        else:
            if not self.values.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
                vals = np.ascontiguousarray(self.values)
                object.__setattr__(self, "values", vals)

        # ---- dates sorted + unique ----
        if T > 1:
            if np.any(self.dates[1:] < self.dates[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("dates must be sorted ascending")
            if np.any(self.dates[1:] == self.dates[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("dates must be unique")

        # ---- factor_ids sorted + unique ----
        if N > 1 and np.any(self.factor_ids[1:] < self.factor_ids[:-1]):
            perm = np.argsort(self.factor_ids, kind="mergesort")
            sorted_ids = self.factor_ids[perm]
            if np.any(sorted_ids[1:] == sorted_ids[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")
            sorted_vals = (
                np.asfortranarray(self.values[:, perm])
                if self.fortran_order
                else np.ascontiguousarray(self.values[:, perm])
            )
            object.__setattr__(self, "factor_ids", sorted_ids)
            object.__setattr__(self, "values", sorted_vals)

    # ---------------------------- Convenience Methods ----------------------------
    def to_df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        index = pd.DatetimeIndex(
            ordinal32_to_datetime64D(self.dates)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else self.dates
        )
        df = pd.DataFrame(
            data=self.values, index=index, columns=self.factor_ids
        )
        df.index.name = "date"
        df.columns.name = "factor_id"
        return df

    def head(self, n: int = 5) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df().head(n=n)

    def tail(self, n: int = 5) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df().tail(n=n)

    def plot(self, **kwargs):
        return self.to_df().plot(**kwargs)

    # ---------------------------- Properties ----------------------------
    @property
    def index_dates(self) -> pd.DatetimeIndex:
        return pd.DatetimeIndex(
            ordinal32_to_datetime64D(self.dates)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else self.dates
        )

    @property
    def index_factors(self) -> pd.Index:
        return pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id")

    @property
    def df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df()

    @property
    def shape(self) -> tuple[int, ...]:
        return self.values.shape

    @property
    def num_dates(self) -> int:
        return self.values.shape[0]

    @property
    def num_factors(self) -> int:
        return self.values.shape[1]

    # ---------------------------- Slicing Methods ----------------------------
    def slice_dates(
        self, start: Optional[int] = None, end: Optional[int] = None
    ) -> "RiskFactorMatrix":
        start = 0 if start is None else start
        end = self.num_dates if end is None else end
        vals = self.values[start:end, :]
        if self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.asfortranarray(vals)
        elif not self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.ascontiguousarray(vals)
        return RiskFactorMatrix(
            dates=self.dates[start:end],
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids,
            values=vals,
            fortran_order=self.fortran_order,
        )

    def slice_factors(self, factor_indices: np.ndarray) -> "RiskFactorMatrix":
        vals = self.values[:, factor_indices]
        if self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.asfortranarray(vals)
        elif not self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.ascontiguousarray(vals)
        return RiskFactorMatrix(
            dates=self.dates,
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids[factor_indices],
            values=vals,
            fortran_order=self.fortran_order,
        )

    # ---------------------------- Time-Series Optimization ----------------------------
    def to_time_series_optimized_array(self) -> np.ndarray:
        """Return F-contiguous array for column-major operations (Numba/Cython)."""
        return (
            np.asfortranarray(self.values)
            if not self.fortran_order
            else self.values
        )

    def get_factor_time_series(self, factor_id: int) -> np.ndarray:
        idx = np.searchsorted(self.factor_ids, factor_id)
        if idx >= self.factor_ids.size or self.factor_ids[idx] != factor_id:
            raise KeyError(f"factor_id {factor_id} not found")
        return np.ascontiguousarray(self.values[:, idx])

    # ---------------------------- Snapshot ----------------------------
    def get_snapshot(self, target_date: date) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
        from riskquant.utils.dates import date_to_ordinal32

        target_val = (
            date_to_ordinal32(target_date)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else np.datetime64(target_date, "D")
        )
        idx = np.searchsorted(self.dates, target_val)
        if idx >= self.dates.size or self.dates[idx] != target_val:
            raise KeyError(f"Date {target_date} not found in matrix")

        levels = self.values[idx, :]
        valid_mask = ~np.isnan(levels)
        return RiskFactorLevelSnapshot(
            as_of_date=target_date,
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids[valid_mask],
            factor_levels=levels[valid_mask],
        )

```

### FILE: market_data\risk_factors\shift_types.py ###
```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/shift_types.py
from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum
from re import S
from typing import Mapping

import numpy as np


class ShiftType(IntEnum):
    NO_SHIFT = -1
    LOG_RETURN = 1
    SIMPLE_RETURN = 2
    DIFFERENCE = 3


# Kernel-friendly int8 constants (avoid repeated casts in hot code)
SHIFT_NO_SHIFT: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.NO_SHIFT)
SHIFT_LOG_RETURN: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.LOG_RETURN)
SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.SIMPLE_RETURN)
SHIFT_DIFFERENCE: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.DIFFERENCE)


# Names tuple
SHIFT_TYPE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in ShiftType
)

# Registry
SHIFT_TYPE_REGISTRY: dict[str, np.int8] = {
    "NO_SHIFT": SHIFT_NO_SHIFT,
    "LOG_RETURN": SHIFT_LOG_RETURN,
    "SIMPLE_RETURN": SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN,
    "DIFFERENCE": SHIFT_DIFFERENCE,
}


def validate_shift_types_dict(shift_types_dict: Mapping[str, int]) -> None:
    """
    Validate that the Excel-provided mapping exactly matches the code enum.

    Rationale:
      - Code is the contract; Excel is data.
      - Kernels rely on stable integer codes.
    """
    # Expected mapping from enum
    expected: dict[str, int] = {
        name: int(member) for name, member in ShiftType.__members__.items()
    }

    # Normalize "got" mapping to plain ints for stable comparison
    got: dict[str, int] = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in shift_types_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "shift_types sheet does not match riskquant ShiftType enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel shift_types table or update riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.shift_types.ShiftType."
    )

```

### FILE: market_data\risk_factors\snapshot.py ###
```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/snapshot.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.utils.dates import ordinal32_to_datetime64D


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """Immutable snapshot of risk factor levels at a single as-of date.

    Attributes
    ----------
    as_of_date : datetime.date
        The calendar date for which the snapshot applies.
    factor_ids : np.ndarray[int32]
        1-D array of unique risk-factor identifiers (dtype int32, C-contiguous).
        Must be sorted ascending; if not sorted, the constructor will re-order
        the ids and corresponding `factor_levels`.
    factor_levels : np.ndarray[float32|float64]
        1-D numeric array of factor levels aligned with `factor_ids`.
        Must be the same length as `factor_ids` and C-contiguous.

    Invariants (enforced on construction)
    -------------------------------------
    - `factor_ids` is 1D, dtype int32, C-contiguous, sorted ascending and unique
    - `factor_levels` is 1D, dtype float32 or float64, C-contiguous
    - lengths of `factor_ids` and `factor_levels` match

    The class is intentionally lightweight and validates eagerly so downstream
    numerical kernels can assume compact, contiguous, and deterministic
    memory layouts (fast gathers, stable hashing, etc.).
    """

    as_of_date: date
    factor_ids: np.ndarray  # (N0,) int32
    factor_levels: np.ndarray  # (N0,) float32/float64

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        """Validate and canonicalize snapshot arrays.

        The method performs cheap, early checks and performs the minimal amount
        of work required to restore canonical invariants (sorting + contiguity).
        It raises clear exceptions on invalid input rather than attempting to
        silently coerce data into a valid form.
        """
        # Local aliases to make subsequent checks concise and explicit.
        ids = self.factor_ids
        lv = self.factor_levels

        # ----- shape checks -----
        # Ensure both arrays are 1-D vectors.
        if ids.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"factor_ids must be 1D; got shape={ids.shape}")
        if lv.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"factor_levels must be 1D; got shape={lv.shape}")

        # Ensure arrays have the same length (alignment requirement).
        if ids.shape[0] != lv.shape[0]:
            raise ValueError(
                f"factor_ids and factor_levels length mismatch: "
                f"{ids.shape[0]} vs {lv.shape[0]}"
            )

        # ----- dtype checks -----
        # factor_ids must be 32-bit integers for compactness and predictable
        # behavior when used as indices or keys in mappings.
        if ids.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"factor_ids must be int32; got {ids.dtype}")

        # factor_levels must be floating point (single or double precision).
        if lv.dtype not in (np.float32, np.float64):
            raise TypeError(
                f"factor_levels must be float32 or float64; got {lv.dtype}"
            )

        # ----- memory layout checks -----
        # Many numerical routines assume C-contiguous 1-D arrays; check early so
        # callers get an explicit error instead of subtle performance bugs.
        if not ids.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be C-contiguous")
        if not lv.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            raise ValueError("factor_levels must be C-contiguous")

        # Fast path: zero or one element requires no further sorting/uniqueness checks.
        n = ids.size
        if n <= 1:
            return

        # ----- sorted + unique enforcement -----
        # If ids are already non-decreasing, perform an adjacent-duplicate check
        # which is allocation-free. If not sorted, build a stable permutation and
        # reorder both arrays so the object is canonical afterwards.
        if not np.any(ids[1:] < ids[:-1]):
            # ids are non-decreasing — ensure strict uniqueness.
            if np.any(ids[1:] == ids[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")
            return

        # ids are unsorted: compute a stable sort permutation (mergesort).
        perm = np.argsort(ids, kind="mergesort")

        # Apply the permutation to ids (produces a new array view/copy).
        ids_sorted = ids[perm]

        # Check for duplicates after sorting (adjacent duplicates are easy to detect).
        if np.any(ids_sorted[1:] == ids_sorted[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")

        # Reorder factor levels to keep alignment with the now-sorted ids.
        lv_sorted = lv[perm]

        # Fancy indexing typically returns a contiguous array for 1-D, but
        # explicitly enforce C-contiguity to preserve the class invariant.
        if not lv_sorted.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            lv_sorted = np.ascontiguousarray(lv_sorted)

        # Bypass dataclass immutability to install the canonicalized arrays.
        object.__setattr__(self, "factor_ids", ids_sorted)
        object.__setattr__(self, "factor_levels", lv_sorted)

    def to_df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Return the snapshot as a 1-row wide DataFrame."""
        # Reshape the 1D levels array into a 2D row vector (1, N)
        data_2d = self.factor_levels[np.newaxis, :]

        df = pd.DataFrame(
            data=data_2d,
            index=pd.DatetimeIndex([self.as_of_date], name="date"),
            columns=pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id"),
        )
        return df

    def to_series(self) -> pd.Series:
        """Return the snapshot as a pandas Series."""
        return pd.Series(
            data=self.factor_levels,
            index=pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id"),
            name=self.as_of_date,
        )

    @property
    def df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Alias for to_df()."""
        return self.to_df()

    @property
    def sr(self) -> pd.Series:
        """Alias for to_series()."""
        return self.to_series()

    @property
    def num_factors(self) -> int:
        return len(self.factor_ids)

```

### FILE: market_data\state.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/state.py

# src/riskquant/market_data/state.py

from dataclasses import dataclass
import numpy as np


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class MarketDataMatrix:
    """
    Vectorized market data container.
    Dimension 0 always corresponds to the CompiledPortfolio UIX spaces.
    Dimension -1 always corresponds to the Scenario Index (num_scenarios).
    """

    num_scenarios: int

    # ========== 1. SCALARS / NODES ==========

    # FX Spot Rates. Shape: (num_fx_factors, num_scenarios)
    # Accessed via: block.fx_under_to_settle_uix and block.fx_strike_to_settle_uix
    fx_spots: np.ndarray

    # Underlying Spot Prices. Shape: (num_price_factors, num_scenarios)
    # Accessed via: block.price_uix (for SpotBlock)
    prices: np.ndarray

    # Forward Prices (Discrete Nodes). Shape: (num_fwd_nodes, num_scenarios)
    # Accessed via: block.price_uix (for ForwardNodeBlock)
    forward_nodes: np.ndarray

    # Volatility (Discrete Nodes). Shape: (num_vol_nodes, num_scenarios)
    # Accessed via: block.vol_uix (for VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock)
    vol_nodes: np.ndarray

    # ========== 2. THE MASTER DISCOUNT GRID ==========
    # Pre-computed Discount Factors for every curve at every unique portfolio date.
    # Shape: (num_discount_curves, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios)
    # Accessed via: market_state.discount_factor_grid[curve_uix, date_uix, :]
    discount_factor_grid: np.ndarray

    # ========== 3. CURVE-BASED DATA (CURVE BLOCKS) ==========

    # Pre-computed Forward Prices for every curve at every unique portfolio date.
    # Shape: (num_price_curves, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios)
    # Accessed via: market_state.price_curve_grid[curve_uix, date_uix, :]
    price_curve_grid: np.ndarray

    # ========== 4. TIME GRIDS ==========
    # Pre-computed Time to Expiry (in years) for every unique portfolio date.
    # Shape: (num_unique_dates,)
    # Accessed via: market_state.tte_grid[block.expiry_date_uix]
    tte_grid: np.ndarray

    # ========== 5. SURFACES ==========
    # The strike/moneyness coordinates for each surface's smile.
    # Shape: (num_vol_surfaces, max_vol_strikes)
    vol_surface_strikes: np.ndarray

    # Pre-computed Time-Sliced Volatility Smiles for every unique portfolio date.
    # Shape: (num_vol_surfaces, num_unique_dates, max_vol_strikes, num_scenarios)
    # Accessed via: market_state.vol_surfaces[block.vol_surface_uix, block.expiry_date_uix, :, :]
    vol_surfaces: np.ndarray

```

```python
from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class SurfaceYAxisType(IntEnum):
    STRIKE = np.int8(1)
    LOG_STRIKE = np.int8(2)
    MONEYNESS_SPOT = np.int8(3)
    MONEYNESS_FWD = np.int8(4)
    LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT = np.int8(5)
    LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD = np.int8(6)
    DELTA_CALL = np.int8(7)


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_STRIKE: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.STRIKE,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_STRIKE: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_STRIKE,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_SPOT: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_SPOT,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_FWD: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_FWD,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.DELTA_CALL,
)

# Names tuple
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in SurfaceYAxisType
)

# Central registry
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_REGISTRY: dict[str, SurfaceYAxisType] = {
    "STRIKE": SurfaceYAxisType.STRIKE,
    "LOG_STRIKE": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_STRIKE,
    "MONEYNESS_SPOT": SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_SPOT,
    "MONEYNESS_FWD": SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_FWD,
    "LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT,
    "LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD,
    "DELTA_CALL": SurfaceYAxisType.DELTA_CALL,
}
```

### FILE: portfolio\blocks\base_block.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/base_block.py


from dataclasses import dataclass
from dataclasses import fields
import numpy as np


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class Block:
    trade_uids: np.ndarray

    # Num trades prop
    @property
    def num_trades(self) -> int:
        return self.trade_uids.shape[0]

    # Print block util
    def print_block(self, max_items: int = 5) -> None:
        cls = type(self).__name__
        print(f"{cls}:")

        for f in fields(self):
            v = getattr(self, f.name)
            if isinstance(v, np.ndarray):
                print(
                    f"  {f.name:<30} | shape: {v.shape!s:>10} | dtype: {v.dtype!s:>10} | values: {v[:max_items]}"
                )
            else:
                print(f"  {f.name:<30} {v}")

```

### FILE: portfolio\blocks\forward_block.py ###
```python
"""
Compiled block for forward trades (struct-of-arrays).

This module defines the compiled runtime representation of forward positions.
Produced by the portfolio compiler from `ForwardPayload` objects, it stores
NumPy arrays of numeric indices into a `CompiledPortfolio` factor universe,
enabling fast vectorized valuation and scenario simulation.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.base_block import Block


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _ForwardBaseBlock(Block):
    """
    Common struct-of-arrays fields shared by all forward blocks.
    """

    # ========== TRADE IDENTITY ==========
    trade_uids: np.ndarray

    # ========== ECONOMIC TERMS ==========
    qty: np.ndarray
    strike: np.ndarray

    # ========== SETTLEMENT DATE ==========
    # (N,) int32 index into unique_dates_ordinal32
    settlement_date_uix: np.ndarray

    # ========== FX MARKET LINKAGES ==========

    # Index into the compiled FX factor universe, or sentinel when no FX needed. dtype: int32 (np.int32)
    fx_under_to_settle_uix: np.ndarray
    fx_strike_to_settle_uix: np.ndarray

    # Flag to indicate if the math needs to invert the FX rate (1/X)
    fx_under_to_settle_invert: np.ndarray  # bool or int8
    fx_strike_to_settle_invert: np.ndarray  # bool or int8

    # ========== DISCOUNT LINKAGES ==========
    under_discount_curve_uix: np.ndarray
    strike_discount_curve_uix: np.ndarray


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class ForwardNodeBlock(_ForwardBaseBlock):
    """
    Struct-of-arrays representation of compiled forward trades
    priced directly off a node/scalar.
    """

    # ========== UNDERLYING MARKET LINKAGE ==========
    # Index into compiled price factor universe for exposure timing.
    price_uix: np.ndarray


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class ForwardCurveBlock(_ForwardBaseBlock):
    """
    Struct-of-arrays representation of compiled forward trades
    priced off a curve requiring delivery date interpolation.
    """

    # ========== DELIVERY DATE ==========
    # (N,) int32 index into unique_dates_ordinal32
    delivery_date_uix: np.ndarray

    # ========== UNDERLYING MARKET LINKAGE ==========
    # Index into compiled price curve universe.
    price_curve_uix: np.ndarray

```

### FILE: portfolio\blocks\spot_block.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/spot_block.py
"""
Compiled, memory-efficient container for spot positions used by the compiled
portfolio runtime.

This module provides `SpotBlock`, a struct-of-arrays representation of a set
of spot trades. The block is intentionally compact (NumPy arrays, primitive
numeric dtypes) so it can be held efficiently in memory and passed between
processes in high-throughput valuation workflows.

Design notes:
  - Row i of every array corresponds to the trade with UID `trade_uids[i]`.
  - Index fields (e.g., `price_uix`, `fx_uix`) are literal static config Risk Factor IDs
    acting as direct integer indices into the centralized `MarketDataMatrix` arrays.
  - FX linkage uses a sentinel index (e.g., -1) to indicate "no FX conversion",
    keeping the representation numeric and avoiding Python object overhead.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass
import numpy as np

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.base_block import Block


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class SpotBlock(Block):
    """
    Struct-of-arrays for compiled spot trades.

    Each attribute is a NumPy array with matching length `m_spot` (number of spot trades).
    The block is frozen and uses slots for memory and performance benefits.

    Row ordering:
      - row i corresponds to trade with UID `trade_uids[i]`.

    Index semantics:
      - `price_uix` is an int32 index into `MarketDataMatrix.prices`.
      - `fx_uix` is an int32 index into `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots` or
        the sentinel value (e.g., -1) indicating no FX conversion is required.
    """

    # ========= TRADE IDENTITY ==========

    # Unique trade identifiers, one per row. dtype: int64 (np.int64)
    trade_uids: np.ndarray

    # ========== ECONOMIC TERMS ==========

    # Signed quantities / notionals for the spot positions. dtype: float64 (np.float64)
    qty: np.ndarray

    # ========== MARKET LINKAGES (UNIVERSE INDICES) ==========

    # Index into the compiled price factor universe for this row. dtype: int32 (np.int32)
    price_uix: np.ndarray

    # Index into the compiled FX factor universe, or sentinel when no FX needed. dtype: int32 (np.int32)
    fx_uix: np.ndarray

    # Flag to indicate if the math needs to invert the FX rate (1/X). dtype: bool (np.bool_)
    fx_invert: np.ndarray

```

### FILE: portfolio\blocks\vanilla_european_option_block.py ###
```python
"""
Compiled block for vanilla European option trades.

This module defines `VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock` and `VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock`,
which are struct-of-arrays representations of option trades optimized for vectorized
valuation and simulation. The blocks store only numeric identifiers and primitive
arrays; pricers and resolvers map these compiled universe indices (uix) to full
market data matrices during runtime.

Key design:
  - Row i across all arrays corresponds to trade UID `trade_uids[i]`.
  - `_uix` fields (e.g., `vol_uix`, `underlying_price_uix`) index into the
    compiled `RiskFactorMatrix` universe, replacing the slower `_rf_id` lookups.
  - Expiry and settlement dates are encoded as int32 days-since-epoch for
    fast array-based interpolation and discounting.
  - `is_call` is packed as an `np.int8` array (1 for call, 0 for put) to minimize
    memory bandwidth during the inner pricing loops.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.base_block import Block


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _VanillaEuropeanOptionBaseBlock(Block):
    """
    Common struct-of-arrays fields shared by all vanilla European option blocks.
    """

    # ========== TRADE IDENTITY ==========

    # Unique trade identifiers (one per row). dtype: int64 (np.int64)
    trade_uids: np.ndarray

    # ========== ECONOMIC TERMS ==========

    # Signed position sizes (contracts / notional). dtype: float64 (np.float64)
    qty: np.ndarray

    # Strike prices K expressed in strike currency units. dtype: float64 (np.float64)
    strike: np.ndarray

    # Option type indicator: 1 => call, 0 => put. dtype: int8 (np.int8)
    is_call: np.ndarray

    # ========== DATES ==========

    # (N,) int32 index into unique_dates_ordinal32
    expiry_date_uix: np.ndarray

    # (N,) int32 index into unique_dates_ordinal32
    settlement_date_uix: np.ndarray

    # ========== FX LINKAGES ==========

    # Index into the compiled FX factor universe, or sentinel when no FX needed. dtype: int32 (np.int32)
    fx_under_to_settle_uix: np.ndarray
    fx_strike_to_settle_uix: np.ndarray

    # Flag to indicate if the math needs to invert the FX rate (1/X)
    fx_under_to_settle_invert: np.ndarray  # bool or int8
    fx_strike_to_settle_invert: np.ndarray  # bool or int8

    # ========== DISCOUNT LINKAGES ==========
    under_discount_curve_uix: np.ndarray
    strike_discount_curve_uix: np.ndarray


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock(_VanillaEuropeanOptionBaseBlock):
    """
    Struct-of-arrays representation of compiled vanilla European options
    where the underlying is sourced from a specific scalar node.
    """

    # ========== MARKET LINKAGES (UNIVERSE INDICES) ==========

    # Index into compiled price factor universe for the underlying asset. dtype: int32
    underlying_price_uix: np.ndarray

    # Index into compiled volatility factor universe (the vol node/series). dtype: int32
    vol_uix: np.ndarray


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock(_VanillaEuropeanOptionBaseBlock):
    """
    Struct-of-arrays representation of compiled vanilla European options
    where the underlying and volatility are sourced from curves/surfaces
    that require date-based interpolation.
    """

    # ========== DATES ==========

    # (N,) int32 index into unique_dates_ordinal32
    delivery_date_uix: np.ndarray

    # ========== MARKET LINKAGES (UNIVERSE INDICES) ==========

    # Index into compiled price curve universe for the underlying asset. dtype: int32
    underlying_price_curve_uix: np.ndarray

    # Index into compiled volatility surface universe. dtype: int32
    vol_surface_uix: np.ndarray

    # Y-Axis type identifier for the vol surface (e.g., strike, log-moneyness). dtype: int8
    vol_surface_y_axis_type_id: np.ndarray

```

### FILE: portfolio\compiled.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/compiled.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Optional, TypeAlias
import numpy as np

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.spot_block import SpotBlock
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.forward_block import (
    ForwardNodeBlock,
    ForwardCurveBlock,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
    VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
    VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
)

# ============================================================
# Type aliases
# ============================================================

BlockType: TypeAlias = (
    SpotBlock
    | ForwardNodeBlock
    | ForwardCurveBlock
    | VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock
    | VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock
)

# ============================================================
# Field order (CRITICAL for performance)
# ============================================================

_FIELD_NAMES = (
    "spot",
    "forward_node",
    "forward_curve",
    "vanilla_european_option_node",
    "vanilla_european_option_curve",
)

# ============================================================
# Runtime container
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class CompiledPortfolio:
    """
    Immutable runtime container of instrument-homogeneous SoA blocks.

    Design:
    - Zero reflection (no dataclasses.fields usage)
    - Predictable field order
    - Fast iteration
    - Minimal Python overhead
    """

    spot: Optional[SpotBlock] = None
    forward_node: Optional[ForwardNodeBlock] = None
    forward_curve: Optional[ForwardCurveBlock] = None
    vanilla_european_option_node: Optional[VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock] = (
        None
    )
    vanilla_european_option_curve: Optional[VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock] = (
        None
    )

    # Temporal coordinates required by the MarketDataMatrix builder
    unique_dates_ordinal32: np.ndarray = field(
        init=True,
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32),
    )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # Fast iteration (no reflection)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    def iter_blocks(self) -> tuple[BlockType, ...]:
        """
        Return all non-None blocks in stable order.
        """
        return tuple(
            block
            for name in _FIELD_NAMES
            if (block := getattr(self, name)) is not None
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # Fast aggregate metrics
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    @property
    def total_trades(self) -> int:
        """
        Total number of trades across all compiled blocks.
        """
        total = 0
        for name in _FIELD_NAMES:
            block = getattr(self, name)
            if block is not None:
                total += block.num_trades
        return total

    # # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # # Constructor helper (avoids kwargs overhead)
    # # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # @classmethod
    # def from_compiled_fields(
    #     cls, compiled_fields: dict[str, BlockType], unique_dates: np.ndarray
    # ) -> "CompiledPortfolio":
    #     """
    #     Fast construction from dict without dynamic kwargs overhead.
    #     """
    #     return cls(
    #         spot=compiled_fields.get("spot"),
    #         forward_node=compiled_fields.get("forward_node"),
    #         forward_curve=compiled_fields.get("forward_curve"),
    #         vanilla_european_option_node=compiled_fields.get(
    #             "vanilla_european_option_node"
    #         ),
    #         vanilla_european_option_curve=compiled_fields.get(
    #             "vanilla_european_option_curve"
    #         ),
    #         unique_dates_ordinal32=unique_dates,
    #     )

```
