### FILE: portfolio\compilers\instruments\forward.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/compilers/instruments/forward.py
from __future__ import annotations
from typing import Optional

from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.forward_block import (
    ForwardNodeBlock,
    ForwardCurveBlock,
)


def compile_forward_node(
    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,
) -> Optional[ForwardNodeBlock]:
    if start == end:
        return None

    return ForwardNodeBlock(
        trade_uids=batch.uids[start:end],
        qty=batch.qty[start:end],
        strike=batch.strike_price[start:end],
        settlement_date_uix=batch.settle_date_uix[start:end],
        fx_under_to_settle_uix=batch.fx_under_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_strike_to_settle_uix=batch.fx_strike_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_under_to_settle_invert=batch.fx_under_to_settle_invert[
            start:end
        ].astype(bool, copy=False),
        fx_strike_to_settle_invert=batch.fx_strike_to_settle_invert[
            start:end
        ].astype(bool, copy=False),
        under_discount_curve_uix=batch.under_discount_curve_ids[start:end],
        strike_discount_curve_uix=batch.strike_discount_curve_ids[start:end],
        price_uix=batch.price_rf_ids[start:end],
    )


def compile_forward_curve(
    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,
) -> Optional[ForwardCurveBlock]:
    if start == end:
        return None

    return ForwardCurveBlock(
        trade_uids=batch.uids[start:end],
        qty=batch.qty[start:end],
        strike=batch.strike_price[start:end],
        settlement_date_uix=batch.settle_date_uix[start:end],
        fx_under_to_settle_uix=batch.fx_under_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_strike_to_settle_uix=batch.fx_strike_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_under_to_settle_invert=batch.fx_under_to_settle_invert[
            start:end
        ].astype(bool, copy=False),
        fx_strike_to_settle_invert=batch.fx_strike_to_settle_invert[
            start:end
        ].astype(bool, copy=False),
        under_discount_curve_uix=batch.under_discount_curve_ids[start:end],
        strike_discount_curve_uix=batch.strike_discount_curve_ids[start:end],
        price_curve_uix=batch.price_curve_ids[start:end],
        delivery_date_uix=batch.delivery_date_uix[start:end],
    )

```

### FILE: portfolio\compilers\instruments\spot.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/compilers/instruments/spot.py
from __future__ import annotations
from typing import Optional

from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.spot_block import SpotBlock


def compile_spot(
    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,
) -> Optional[SpotBlock]:
    if start == end:
        return None

    # Slicing a contiguous numpy array [start:end] returns a view.
    # Zero memory copy. O(1) CPU time.
    return SpotBlock(
        trade_uids=batch.uids[start:end],
        qty=batch.qty[start:end],
        price_uix=batch.price_rf_ids[start:end],
        fx_uix=batch.fx_under_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_invert=batch.fx_under_to_settle_invert[start:end].astype(
            bool, copy=False
        ),
    )

```

### FILE: portfolio\compilers\instruments\vanilla_european_option.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/compilers/instruments/vanilla_european_option.py
from __future__ import annotations
from typing import Optional

from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
    VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
    VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
)


def compile_vanilla_euro_option_node(
    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,
) -> Optional[VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock]:
    if start == end:
        return None

    return VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock(
        trade_uids=batch.uids[start:end],
        qty=batch.qty[start:end],
        strike=batch.strike_price[start:end],
        is_call=batch.is_call[start:end],
        expiry_date_uix=batch.expiry_date_uix[start:end],
        settlement_date_uix=batch.settle_date_uix[start:end],
        fx_under_to_settle_uix=batch.fx_under_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_strike_to_settle_uix=batch.fx_strike_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_under_to_settle_invert=batch.fx_under_to_settle_invert[
            start:end
        ].astype(bool, copy=False),
        fx_strike_to_settle_invert=batch.fx_strike_to_settle_invert[
            start:end
        ].astype(bool, copy=False),
        under_discount_curve_uix=batch.under_discount_curve_ids[start:end],
        strike_discount_curve_uix=batch.strike_discount_curve_ids[start:end],
        underlying_price_uix=batch.price_rf_ids[start:end],
        vol_uix=batch.vol_rf_ids[start:end],
    )


def compile_vanilla_euro_option_curve(
    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,
) -> Optional[VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock]:
    if start == end:
        return None

    return VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock(
        trade_uids=batch.uids[start:end],
        qty=batch.qty[start:end],
        strike=batch.strike_price[start:end],
        is_call=batch.is_call[start:end],
        expiry_date_uix=batch.expiry_date_uix[start:end],
        settlement_date_uix=batch.settle_date_uix[start:end],
        fx_under_to_settle_uix=batch.fx_under_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_strike_to_settle_uix=batch.fx_strike_to_settle_ids[start:end],
        fx_under_to_settle_invert=batch.fx_under_to_settle_invert[
            start:end
        ].astype(bool, copy=False),
        fx_strike_to_settle_invert=batch.fx_strike_to_settle_invert[
            start:end
        ].astype(bool, copy=False),
        under_discount_curve_uix=batch.under_discount_curve_ids[start:end],
        strike_discount_curve_uix=batch.strike_discount_curve_ids[start:end],
        underlying_price_curve_uix=batch.price_curve_ids[start:end],
        delivery_date_uix=batch.delivery_date_uix[start:end],
        vol_surface_uix=batch.vol_surface_rf_ids[start:end],
        vol_surface_y_axis_type_id=batch.vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids[start:end],
    )

```

### FILE: portfolio\compilers\registry.py ###
```python
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Callable, Final, Optional, TypeAlias

from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch

from riskquant.portfolio.compilers.instruments.spot import compile_spot
from riskquant.portfolio.compilers.instruments.forward import (
    compile_forward_curve,
    compile_forward_node,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.compilers.instruments.vanilla_european_option import (
    compile_vanilla_euro_option_curve,
    compile_vanilla_euro_option_node,
)

from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import (
    NUM_INSTRUMENTS,
    SPOT_ID,
    FORWARD_NODE_ID,
    FORWARD_CURVE_ID,
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID,
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID,
)

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.spot_block import SpotBlock
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.forward_block import (
    ForwardNodeBlock,
    ForwardCurveBlock,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
    VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
    VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
)

# ============================================================
# Type aliases
# ============================================================

BlockType: TypeAlias = (
    SpotBlock
    | ForwardNodeBlock
    | ForwardCurveBlock
    | VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock
    | VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock
)

CompilerFn: TypeAlias = Callable[
    [IngestBatch, int, int],
    Optional[BlockType],
]

# ============================================================
# Dispatch tables (GOD-TIER)
# ============================================================

# If instrument IDs are small integers → this is fastest possible dispatch

_MAX_INSTRUMENT_ID: Final = NUM_INSTRUMENTS

COMPILERS: Final[list[Optional[CompilerFn]]] = [None] * _MAX_INSTRUMENT_ID
FIELD_NAMES: Final[list[Optional[str]]] = [None] * _MAX_INSTRUMENT_ID


def _register(instrument_id: int, field_name: str, fn: CompilerFn) -> None:
    COMPILERS[instrument_id] = fn
    FIELD_NAMES[instrument_id] = field_name


# ============================================================
# Registration (executed once at import)
# ============================================================

# ---> SPOT
_register(
    int(SPOT_ID),
    "spot",
    compile_spot,
)

# ---> FORWARDS
_register(
    int(FORWARD_NODE_ID),
    "forward_node",
    compile_forward_node,
)
_register(
    int(FORWARD_CURVE_ID),
    "forward_curve",
    compile_forward_curve,
)

# ---> VANILLA EUROPEAN OPTIONS
_register(
    int(VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID),
    "vanilla_european_option_node",
    compile_vanilla_euro_option_node,
)
_register(
    int(VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID),
    "vanilla_european_option_curve",
    compile_vanilla_euro_option_curve,
)

# ---> REGISTER MORE INSTRUMENTS HERE... (if instrument_id < _MAX_INSTRUMENT_ID)

```

### FILE: portfolio\engine.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/engine.py

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

from riskquant.portfolio.compilers.registry import COMPILERS, FIELD_NAMES
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch
from riskquant.portfolio.compiled import CompiledPortfolio


def compile_portfolio_from_batch(batch: IngestBatch) -> CompiledPortfolio:
    instrument_ids = batch.instrument_ids
    n = instrument_ids.size

    if n == 0:
        return CompiledPortfolio()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # 1. Sort once
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    order = np.argsort(instrument_ids, kind="stable")
    batch = batch.reorder(order)

    sorted_ids = batch.instrument_ids

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # 2. Find boundaries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    change_idx = np.flatnonzero(sorted_ids[1:] != sorted_ids[:-1]) + 1
    boundaries = np.concatenate(([0], change_idx, [n]))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # 3. Compile blocks
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    compiled_fields = {}

    # Local binding for faster access inside loop
    compilers = COMPILERS
    field_names = FIELD_NAMES

    n_groups = len(boundaries) - 1

    for i in range(n_groups):
        start = boundaries[i]
        end = boundaries[i + 1]

        instrument_id = sorted_ids[start]

        compiler = compilers[instrument_id]
        if compiler is None:
            continue

        field_name = field_names[instrument_id]

        block = compiler(batch, start, end)

        if block is not None:
            compiled_fields[field_name] = block

    # Pass the unique dates reference directly into the container mapping
    compiled_fields["unique_dates_ordinal32"] = batch.unique_dates_ordinal32

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # 4. Construct result
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    return CompiledPortfolio(**compiled_fields)

```

### FILE: portfolio\fx.py ###
```python
# riskquant/portfolio/fx.py
from __future__ import annotations

import re
from typing import Iterable

_ccy_re = re.compile(r"^[A-Z]{3}$")


def validate_ccy(ccy: str) -> str:
    if not isinstance(ccy, str):
        raise TypeError(f"Currency must be str; got {type(ccy).__name__}")
    if len(ccy) != 3:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Currency must be exactly 3 letters; got '{ccy}' ({len(ccy)})"
        )
    if _ccy_re.match(ccy) is None:
        raise ValueError(f"Currency must be 3 uppercase letters A–Z; got '{ccy}'")
    return ccy


def validate_ccys_unique(ccys: Iterable[str]) -> None:
    """Validate each distinct CCY once; fail fast on first invalid."""
    for c in set(ccys):
        validate_ccy(c)


def get_fx_pair_for_under_to_settle_conversion(
    *,
    underlying_ccy: str,
    settlement_ccy: str,
) -> str:
    # Validate scalars only (no allocations)
    u = validate_ccy(underlying_ccy)
    s = validate_ccy(settlement_ccy)
    if u == s:
        return ""
    return f"{u}{s}"

```

### FILE: portfolio\ingest\batch.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/batch.py
"""
Struct-of-Arrays (SoA) container for a validated batch of trades.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass
import numpy as np
from pandas import DataFrame, Series, NaT

from riskquant.constants import (
    NO_DISCOUNT_CURVE_ID,
    NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED,
    NO_DELIVERY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    NO_SETTLEMENT_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    NO_EXPIRY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    NOT_OPTION,
)
from riskquant.portfolio import fx
from riskquant.utils.dates import ordinal32_to_datetime64D


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class IngestBatch:
    """
    Pristine, contiguous array container for trade ingestion.
    """

    # Number of trades in this batch
    size: int

    # ========== ROUTING / DISPATCH ==========
    instrument_ids: np.ndarray

    # ========== CORE IDENTITY & ECONOMICS ==========
    uids: np.ndarray
    qty: np.ndarray
    strike_price: np.ndarray
    is_call: np.ndarray

    # ========== DATES (ORDINAL32) ==========
    settle_ord: np.ndarray
    delivery_ord: np.ndarray
    expiry_ord: np.ndarray

    # Unique set of all relevant dates across the batch, mapped to a compact UIX space for curve lookups.
    unique_dates_ordinal32: np.ndarray

    # ========= DATES UIX =========

    # UIX arrays for the settlement, delivery, and expiry dates of each trade. These map into the unique_dates_ordinal32 array.
    settle_date_uix: np.ndarray
    delivery_date_uix: np.ndarray
    expiry_date_uix: np.ndarray

    # ========== MARKET & FX LINKAGES (UNIVERSE INDICES) ==========
    fx_under_to_settle_ids: np.ndarray
    fx_under_to_settle_invert: np.ndarray
    fx_strike_to_settle_ids: np.ndarray
    fx_strike_to_settle_invert: np.ndarray
    price_rf_ids: np.ndarray
    price_curve_ids: np.ndarray
    vol_rf_ids: np.ndarray
    vol_surface_rf_ids: np.ndarray
    under_discount_curve_ids: np.ndarray
    strike_discount_curve_ids: np.ndarray
    vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids: np.ndarray

    def to_df(self) -> DataFrame:
        """
        Reconstructs a Pandas DataFrame from the SoA arrays for auditing and debugging.
        Translates ordinals back to datetimes and applies NaT/NaN for sentinels.
        """

        def decode_dates(ord_array: np.ndarray, sentinel: int) -> Series:
            mask = ord_array == sentinel
            dts = ordinal32_to_datetime64D(ord_array)
            s = Series(dts)
            s[mask] = NaT
            return s

        def mask_fx(fx_array: np.ndarray) -> Series:
            s = Series(fx_array, dtype=object)
            s[fx_array == NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED] = "NONE"
            return s

        def mask_fx_invert(fx_invert_array: np.ndarray) -> Series:
            s = Series(fx_invert_array, dtype=bool)
            return s

        def mask_curve(curve_array: np.ndarray) -> Series:
            s = Series(curve_array, dtype=object)
            s[curve_array == NO_DISCOUNT_CURVE_ID] = "NONE"
            return s

        return DataFrame(
            {
                # IDS
                "uid": self.uids,
                "instrument_id": self.instrument_ids,
                # ECONOMICS
                "qty": self.qty,
                "strike_price": Series(self.strike_price).replace(0.0, np.nan),
                "is_call": Series(self.is_call).replace(NOT_OPTION, np.nan),
                # LINKAGES
                "price_rf_id": self.price_rf_ids,
                "price_curve_id": self.price_curve_ids,
                "vol_rf_id": self.vol_rf_ids,
                "vol_surface_rf_id": self.vol_surface_rf_ids,
                "fx_u2s": mask_fx(self.fx_under_to_settle_ids),
                "fx_u2s_invert": mask_fx_invert(self.fx_under_to_settle_invert),
                "fx_k2s": mask_fx(self.fx_strike_to_settle_ids),
                "fx_k2s_invert": mask_fx_invert(
                    self.fx_strike_to_settle_invert
                ),
                "under_discount_curve_id": mask_curve(
                    self.under_discount_curve_ids
                ),
                "strike_discount_curve_id": mask_curve(
                    self.strike_discount_curve_ids
                ),
                # DATES
                "expiry_date": decode_dates(
                    self.expiry_ord,
                    NO_EXPIRY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
                ),
                "delivery_date": decode_dates(
                    self.delivery_ord,
                    NO_DELIVERY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
                ),
                "settle_date": decode_dates(
                    self.settle_ord,
                    NO_SETTLEMENT_DATE_ORDINAL32,
                ),
                # DATE GRID FIELDS
                "settle_date_uix": self.settle_date_uix,
                "delivery_date_uix": self.delivery_date_uix,
                "expiry_date_uix": self.expiry_date_uix,
                # Volatility Surface Y-Axis Type
                "vol_surface_y_axis_type_id": self.vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids,
            }
        )

    @property
    def df(self) -> DataFrame:
        """
        Alias for .to_df() to allow easy access to the reconstructed DataFrame.
        """
        return self.to_df()

    def reorder(
        self,
        order: np.ndarray,
    ) -> "IngestBatch":
        """
        Returns a new IngestBatch with all arrays reordered according to the provided index array.
        This is useful for sorting or grouping trades while maintaining the SoA structure.
        """
        return IngestBatch(
            size=self.size,
            instrument_ids=self.instrument_ids[order],
            uids=self.uids[order],
            qty=self.qty[order],
            strike_price=self.strike_price[order],
            is_call=self.is_call[order],
            settle_ord=self.settle_ord[order],
            delivery_ord=self.delivery_ord[order],
            expiry_ord=self.expiry_ord[order],
            unique_dates_ordinal32=self.unique_dates_ordinal32,  # Unchanged
            settle_date_uix=self.settle_date_uix[order],
            delivery_date_uix=self.delivery_date_uix[order],
            expiry_date_uix=self.expiry_date_uix[order],
            fx_under_to_settle_ids=self.fx_under_to_settle_ids[order],
            fx_under_to_settle_invert=self.fx_under_to_settle_invert[order],
            fx_strike_to_settle_ids=self.fx_strike_to_settle_ids[order],
            fx_strike_to_settle_invert=self.fx_strike_to_settle_invert[order],
            price_rf_ids=self.price_rf_ids[order],
            price_curve_ids=self.price_curve_ids[order],
            vol_rf_ids=self.vol_rf_ids[order],
            vol_surface_rf_ids=self.vol_surface_rf_ids[order],
            under_discount_curve_ids=self.under_discount_curve_ids[order],
            strike_discount_curve_ids=self.strike_discount_curve_ids[order],
            vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids=(
                self.vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids[order]
            ),
        )

```

### FILE: portfolio\ingest\batch_builder.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/batch_builder.py
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import (
    Instrument,
    FORWARD_CURVE_ID,
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID,
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID,
    SPOT_ID,
)
from riskquant.constants import (
    NO_DATE_UIX,
    NO_PRICE_ID,
    NO_PRICE_CURVE_ID,
    NO_VOL_ID,
    NO_DISCOUNT_CURVE_ID,
    NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED,
    NO_DELIVERY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    NO_SETTLEMENT_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    NO_EXPIRY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    NOT_OPTION,
    VOL_RISK_FACTOR_PREFIX,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.schema import REQUIRED_BASE_COLS
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.validator import (
    require_cols_in_df,
    require_non_nan_in_series,
    require_non_zero_values,
    require_positive_values,
    validate_instruments,
    validate_is_call_flags,
    validate_conditional_option_fields,
    validate_uids_unique,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.parser import (
    parse_optional_date_series_to_ordinal32,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.fx_resolver import resolve_fx_rf_ids_inplace
from riskquant.market_data.surfaces.y_axes_types import (
    SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_REGISTRY,
)


def build_ingest_batch(
    *,
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    rf_name_to_rf_id: dict[str, int],
    container_name_to_container_id: dict[str, int],
    rf_id_to_ccy: dict[int, str],
    container_id_to_ccy: dict[int, str],
    container_name_to_y_axis_type_name: dict[str, str],
) -> IngestBatch:
    n = len(df)
    if n == 0:
        raise ValueError("Cannot build IngestBatch from an empty DataFrame.")

    # ==========================================
    # 1. BULK VALIDATION ("SHIFT LEFT")
    # ==========================================
    require_cols_in_df(df, REQUIRED_BASE_COLS, ctx="Positions Ingest")
    for col in REQUIRED_BASE_COLS:
        require_non_nan_in_series(df[col], ctx=f"Column '{col}'")

    validate_instruments(df["instrument"], ctx="Instrument Validation")
    validate_conditional_option_fields(df, ctx="Option Fields Validation")
    validate_uids_unique(df["uid"], ctx="UID Validation")
    require_non_zero_values(df["quantity"], ctx="Quantity Validation")

    if "is_call" in df.columns:
        validate_is_call_flags(df["is_call"], ctx="is_call Validation")
    if "strike_price" in df.columns:
        require_positive_values(
            df["strike_price"], ctx="Strike Price Validation"
        )

    # ==========================================
    # 2. TRANSLATE INSTRUMENTS & UIDs
    # ==========================================
    uids = df["uid"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64)
    qty = df["quantity"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)

    instrument_ids = np.array(
        [Instrument[inst].value for inst in df["instrument"]],
        dtype=np.int32,
    )

    # ==========================================
    # 3. TRANSLATE RISK FACTOR MAPPINGS
    # ==========================================
    # OPTIMIZATION: Integer logic instead of string matching
    is_container_mask = (instrument_ids == FORWARD_CURVE_ID) | (
        instrument_ids == VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID
    )
    is_option_mask = (instrument_ids == VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID) | (
        instrument_ids == VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID
    )

    # --- PRICE MAPPINGS ---
    node_underlyings = df.loc[~is_container_mask, "underlying"]
    mapped_nodes = node_underlyings.map(rf_name_to_rf_id)
    require_non_nan_in_series(mapped_nodes, ctx="Mapped Node Underlying IDs")

    container_underlyings = df.loc[is_container_mask, "underlying"]
    mapped_containers = container_underlyings.map(
        container_name_to_container_id
    )
    require_non_nan_in_series(mapped_containers, ctx="Mapped Container IDs")

    price_rf_ids = np.full(n, NO_PRICE_ID, dtype=np.int32)
    price_curve_ids = np.full(n, NO_PRICE_CURVE_ID, dtype=np.int32)
    vol_rf_ids = np.full(n, NO_VOL_ID, dtype=np.int32)
    vol_surface_rf_ids = np.full(n, NO_VOL_ID, dtype=np.int32)

    price_rf_ids[~is_container_mask] = mapped_nodes.to_numpy(dtype=np.int32)
    price_curve_ids[is_container_mask] = mapped_containers.to_numpy(
        dtype=np.int32
    )

    # DEFAULT Fallback mapping config
    default_y_axis = SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_REGISTRY["STRIKE"].value
    vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids = np.full(n, default_y_axis, dtype=np.int8)

    # --- VOLATILITY MAPPINGS (Options Only) ---
    if is_option_mask.any():
        option_nodes_mask = is_option_mask & ~is_container_mask
        option_curves_mask = is_option_mask & is_container_mask

        if option_nodes_mask.any():
            vol_nodes = (
                VOL_RISK_FACTOR_PREFIX + df.loc[option_nodes_mask, "underlying"]
            )
            mapped_vol_nodes = vol_nodes.map(rf_name_to_rf_id)
            require_non_nan_in_series(
                mapped_vol_nodes, ctx="Mapped Vol Node IDs"
            )
            vol_rf_ids[option_nodes_mask] = mapped_vol_nodes.to_numpy(
                dtype=np.int32
            )

        if option_curves_mask.any():
            vol_curves = (
                VOL_RISK_FACTOR_PREFIX
                + df.loc[option_curves_mask, "underlying"]
            )
            mapped_vol_curves = vol_curves.map(container_name_to_container_id)
            require_non_nan_in_series(
                mapped_vol_curves, ctx="Mapped Vol Surface IDs"
            )
            vol_surface_rf_ids[option_curves_mask] = mapped_vol_curves.to_numpy(
                dtype=np.int32
            )

            mapped_y_axis_type_strs = vol_curves.map(
                container_name_to_y_axis_type_name
            )
            mapped_y_axis_type_ids = mapped_y_axis_type_strs.map(
                lambda x: (
                    SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_REGISTRY[x.upper()].value
                    if pd.notna(x)
                    else default_y_axis
                )
            )
            vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids[option_curves_mask] = (
                mapped_y_axis_type_ids.to_numpy(dtype=np.int8)
            )

    # ==========================================
    # 4. HANDLE OPTIONAL COLUMNS (Economics)
    # ==========================================
    if "strike_price" in df.columns:
        strike_price = df["strike_price"].fillna(0.0).to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        strike_price = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if "is_call" in df.columns:
        is_call = df["is_call"].fillna(NOT_OPTION).to_numpy(dtype=np.int8)
    else:
        is_call = np.full(n, NOT_OPTION, dtype=np.int8)

    # ==========================================
    # 5. PARSE DATES TO ORDINAL32
    # ==========================================
    delivery_ord = parse_optional_date_series_to_ordinal32(
        df.get("delivery_date", pd.Series(index=df.index, dtype=object)),
        col_name="delivery_date",
        sentinel=NO_DELIVERY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    )
    settle_ord = parse_optional_date_series_to_ordinal32(
        df.get("settlement_date", pd.Series(index=df.index, dtype=object)),
        col_name="settlement_date",
        sentinel=NO_SETTLEMENT_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    )
    expiry_ord = parse_optional_date_series_to_ordinal32(
        df.get("expiry_date", pd.Series(index=df.index, dtype=object)),
        col_name="expiry_date",
        sentinel=NO_EXPIRY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
    )

    # ==========================================
    # 6. RESOLVE FX LINKAGES IN BULK
    # ==========================================
    underlying_ccy_series = pd.Series(index=df.index, dtype=object)
    underlying_ccy_series.loc[~is_container_mask] = mapped_nodes.map(
        rf_id_to_ccy
    )
    underlying_ccy_series.loc[is_container_mask] = mapped_containers.map(
        container_id_to_ccy
    )

    require_non_nan_in_series(
        underlying_ccy_series, ctx="Underlying Currency Config Resolution"
    )

    underlying_ccy = underlying_ccy_series.to_numpy(dtype=object)
    settlement_ccy = df["settlement_ccy"].to_numpy(dtype=object)

    if "strike_ccy" in df.columns:
        strike_ccy = (
            df["strike_ccy"]
            .fillna(underlying_ccy_series)
            .to_numpy(dtype=object)
        )
    else:
        strike_ccy = underlying_ccy

    fx_under_to_settle_ids = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    fx_under_to_settle_invert = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    resolve_fx_rf_ids_inplace(
        base_ccy=underlying_ccy,
        settlement_ccy=settlement_ccy,
        out_fx_rf_ids=fx_under_to_settle_ids,
        out_fx_invert=fx_under_to_settle_invert,
        rf_name_to_rf_id=rf_name_to_rf_id,
    )

    fx_strike_to_settle_ids = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    fx_strike_to_settle_invert = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    resolve_fx_rf_ids_inplace(
        base_ccy=strike_ccy,
        settlement_ccy=settlement_ccy,
        out_fx_rf_ids=fx_strike_to_settle_ids,
        out_fx_invert=fx_strike_to_settle_invert,
        rf_name_to_rf_id=rf_name_to_rf_id,
    )

    # ==========================================
    # 6.1 CLEAN UP NON-STRIKE INSTRUMENTS
    # ==========================================
    is_spot_mask = instrument_ids == SPOT_ID
    fx_strike_to_settle_ids[is_spot_mask] = NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED
    fx_strike_to_settle_invert[is_spot_mask] = 0

    # ==========================================
    # 6.5 RESOLVE DISCOUNT CURVES (TRADE LEVEL)
    # ==========================================
    if "under_discount_curve" in df.columns:
        mapped_under_dc = df["under_discount_curve"].map(
            container_name_to_container_id
        )
        under_discount_curve_ids = mapped_under_dc.fillna(
            NO_DISCOUNT_CURVE_ID
        ).to_numpy(dtype=np.int32)
    else:
        under_discount_curve_ids = np.full(
            n, NO_DISCOUNT_CURVE_ID, dtype=np.int32
        )

    if "strike_discount_curve" in df.columns:
        mapped_strike_dc = df["strike_discount_curve"].map(
            container_name_to_container_id
        )
        strike_discount_curve_ids = mapped_strike_dc.fillna(
            NO_DISCOUNT_CURVE_ID
        ).to_numpy(dtype=np.int32)
    else:
        strike_discount_curve_ids = np.full(
            n, NO_DISCOUNT_CURVE_ID, dtype=np.int32
        )

    # ==========================================
    # 7. EXTRACT UNIQUE DATES & MAP TO UIX GRID
    # ==========================================
    all_dates = np.concatenate([settle_ord, delivery_ord, expiry_ord])

    valid_mask = (
        (all_dates != NO_SETTLEMENT_DATE_ORDINAL32)
        & (all_dates != NO_DELIVERY_DATE_ORDINAL32)
        & (all_dates != NO_EXPIRY_DATE_ORDINAL32)
    )

    unique_dates_ordinal32 = np.unique(all_dates[valid_mask])

    settle_date_uix = np.where(
        settle_ord == NO_SETTLEMENT_DATE_ORDINAL32,
        NO_DATE_UIX,
        np.searchsorted(unique_dates_ordinal32, settle_ord),
    ).astype(np.int32)

    delivery_date_uix = np.where(
        delivery_ord == NO_DELIVERY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
        NO_DATE_UIX,
        np.searchsorted(unique_dates_ordinal32, delivery_ord),
    ).astype(np.int32)

    expiry_date_uix = np.where(
        expiry_ord == NO_EXPIRY_DATE_ORDINAL32,
        NO_DATE_UIX,
        np.searchsorted(unique_dates_ordinal32, expiry_ord),
    ).astype(np.int32)

    # ==========================================
    # 8. PACK THE DUMB BATCH
    # ==========================================
    return IngestBatch(
        size=n,
        instrument_ids=instrument_ids,
        uids=uids,
        qty=qty,
        strike_price=strike_price,
        is_call=is_call,
        settle_ord=settle_ord,
        delivery_ord=delivery_ord,
        expiry_ord=expiry_ord,
        fx_under_to_settle_ids=fx_under_to_settle_ids,
        fx_strike_to_settle_ids=fx_strike_to_settle_ids,
        fx_under_to_settle_invert=fx_under_to_settle_invert,
        fx_strike_to_settle_invert=fx_strike_to_settle_invert,
        price_rf_ids=price_rf_ids,
        price_curve_ids=price_curve_ids,
        vol_rf_ids=vol_rf_ids,
        vol_surface_rf_ids=vol_surface_rf_ids,
        under_discount_curve_ids=under_discount_curve_ids,
        strike_discount_curve_ids=strike_discount_curve_ids,
        unique_dates_ordinal32=unique_dates_ordinal32,
        settle_date_uix=settle_date_uix,
        delivery_date_uix=delivery_date_uix,
        expiry_date_uix=expiry_date_uix,
        vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids=vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids,
    )

```

### FILE: portfolio\ingest\fx_resolver.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/fx_resolver.py
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.exceptions import PrettyKeyError
from riskquant.constants import NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED


def resolve_fx_rf_ids_inplace(
    *,
    base_ccy: np.ndarray,  # (n,) object/str
    settlement_ccy: np.ndarray,  # (n,) object/str
    out_fx_rf_ids: np.ndarray,  # (n,) int32
    out_fx_invert: np.ndarray,  # (n,) int32
    rf_name_to_rf_id: dict[str, int],
) -> None:
    """
    Vectorized resolution of FX RF IDs for rows where base_ccy != settlement_ccy.
    Completely eliminates Python for-loops and regex string matching.
    """
    # Initialize default states (No FX required, not inverted)
    out_fx_rf_ids.fill(np.int32(NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED))
    out_fx_invert.fill(0)

    # Get a boolean mask for rows where base_ccy != settlement_ccy
    mask = base_ccy != settlement_ccy

    # Early exit if there are no rows that require FX conversion.
    if not np.any(mask):
        return

    # Extract strings for mismatched rows
    b_str = base_ccy[mask].astype(str)
    s_str = settlement_ccy[mask].astype(str)

    # Fast vectorized string concatenation for currency pairs
    std_pairs = np.char.add(b_str, s_str)
    inv_pairs = np.char.add(s_str, b_str)

    # Map directly via Pandas (C-level hash lookup)
    std_mapped = pd.Series(std_pairs).map(rf_name_to_rf_id).to_numpy()
    inv_mapped = pd.Series(inv_pairs).map(rf_name_to_rf_id).to_numpy()

    # Identify which mapping succeeded
    std_valid = ~np.isnan(std_mapped)
    inv_valid = ~np.isnan(inv_mapped)

    # Find failures
    missing_mask = ~(std_valid | inv_valid)
    if np.any(missing_mask):
        bad_b = b_str[missing_mask][0]
        bad_s = s_str[missing_mask][0]
        raise PrettyKeyError(
            "Missing FX spot risk factor for currency conversion:\n"
            f"- Searched for standard: '{bad_b}{bad_s}'\n"
            f"- Searched for inverted: '{bad_s}{bad_b}'\n"
            f"- base_ccy='{bad_b}'\n"
            f"- settlement_ccy='{bad_s}'\n"
        )

    # Use standard where valid, else fallback to inverted
    final_ids = np.where(std_valid, std_mapped, inv_mapped).astype(np.int32)
    final_inverts = np.where(std_valid, 0, 1).astype(np.int32)

    # Slot back into output arrays
    out_fx_rf_ids[mask] = final_ids
    out_fx_invert[mask] = final_inverts

```

### FILE: portfolio\ingest\parser.py ###
```python
# src\riskquant\portfolio\ingest\parser.py


from __future__ import annotations
from datetime import date, datetime

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas.api.types import (
    is_datetime64_any_dtype,
)

from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.utils import is_na_helper
from riskquant.utils.dates import (
    datetime64D_to_ordinal32,
    is_date_str_valid,
)


def parse_optional_date_series_to_ordinal32(
    ser: pd.Series,
    *,
    col_name: str,
    sentinel: np.int32,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Convert a date-like column to ordinal32 days since DATE_EPOCH using the canonical
    riskquant date encoding (datetime64[D] -> ordinal32).

    Accepted inputs:
      - NaN / None / ""        -> sentinel
      - datetime64 / Timestamp -> encoded via datetime64D_to_ordinal32
      - str "YYYY-MM-DD"       -> validated via is_date_str_valid then encoded
    """
    # Number of rows in the input series
    n = int(len(ser))

    # Initialize the output array with the sentinel value
    out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    out.fill(np.int32(sentinel))

    # Early exit if the series is empty, since the rest of the code assumes at least one row
    if n == 0:
        return out

    # Convert the series to a numpy array for efficient access, without copying if possible
    date_vals = ser.to_numpy(copy=False)

    # Create a boolean mask for non-NA values.
    nn_mask = pd.notna(date_vals)

    # If the dtype is object, we also want to treat empty strings as NA.
    if date_vals.dtype == object:
        date_vals_bind = date_vals  # local binding for efficiency
        nn_mask_bind = nn_mask  # local binding for efficiency
        for i in range(n):
            if nn_mask_bind[i]:
                v = date_vals_bind[i]
                if isinstance(v, str) and (v == "" or v.isspace()):
                    nn_mask_bind[i] = False

    # If there are no non-NA values, we can return early with the all-sentinel output.
    if not np.any(nn_mask):
        return out

    # If the dtype of the series is timezone-aware datetime, we should raise an error since we don't support timezones in our date encoding.
    if isinstance(ser.dtype, pd.DatetimeTZDtype):
        raise TypeError(
            f"Invalid {col_name}: timezone-aware datetimes are not supported."
        )

    # If the dtype of the non-NA values is already datetime64, we can convert them directly to ordinal32 without having to iterate over them.
    if is_datetime64_any_dtype(ser.dtype):
        # Convert the series to datetime64[D] without copying if possible.
        d64 = date_vals.astype("datetime64[D]", copy=False)
        out[nn_mask] = datetime64D_to_ordinal32(d64[nn_mask])
        return out

    # Get the indices of the non-NA values
    idx = np.nonzero(nn_mask)[0]

    # Pre-allocate a datetime64[D] array.
    d64 = np.empty(idx.shape[0], dtype="datetime64[D]")

    # Local bindings for efficiency in the loop
    _is_na = is_na_helper
    _is_date_str_valid = is_date_str_valid

    # Iterate over the non-NA values and parse each one according to its type.
    for k, i in enumerate(idx):
        # Get the original value from the series for this index.
        x = date_vals[i]

        # Smoke test! If the value is NA (None, NaN, or empty string), we should have already marked it as NA in the mask, so we can skip it.
        if _is_na(x):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Internal error: encountered NA value at row {int(i)} that was not marked as NA in the mask. Value: {x}"
            )

        # Now we know x is not NA, so we can parse it according to the accepted input types.
        if isinstance(x, (pd.Timestamp, np.datetime64, datetime, date)):
            d64[k] = np.datetime64(x, "D")
            continue

        # If the dtype of the series is object, we may have string values that need to be validated and parsed.
        if isinstance(x, str):
            xs = x.strip()
            if not _is_date_str_valid(xs):
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Invalid {col_name}='{xs}' at row {int(i)}!"
                    f"Got a string that is not a valid date in YYYY-MM-DD format: '{xs}'. "
                    "Expected strict YYYY-MM-DD."
                )
            d64[k] = np.datetime64(xs, "D")
            continue

        # If we get here, it means x is not NA and not one of the accepted types, so we will raise an error with a helpful message.
        raise TypeError(
            f"Invalid {col_name} type at row {int(i)}: value = {x}, type = {type(x).__name__}. "
            "Expected YYYY-MM-DD string, pandas Timestamp, datetime, or date."
        )

    # At this point, we have successfully parsed all the non-NA values into the d64 array as datetime64[D]. We can now convert them to ordinal32 and store them in the output array at the correct indices.
    out[idx] = datetime64D_to_ordinal32(d64)

    # Return.
    return out

```

### FILE: portfolio\ingest\utils.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/utils.py

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def is_na_helper(x: object) -> bool:
    """Check if a scalar value is missing or invalid (None, NaN, pd.NA, ±inf)."""
    if x is None:
        # None is considered missing.
        return True

    if x is pd.NA:
        # pd.NA is considered missing.
        return True

    if isinstance(x, (float, np.floating)):
        # Check for NaN or infinite values.
        # np.isfinite returns False for NaN and ±inf, so we return True for those cases.
        # NaNs and infinite values are considered missing/invalid.
        return not np.isfinite(x)

    # For all other types (e.g., int, str), we consider them valid and not missing.
    return False

```

### FILE: portfolio\ingest\validator.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/validator.py

from typing import Iterable
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE
from riskquant.exceptions import PrettyKeyError
from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import Instrument
from riskquant.utils.printer import as_list_str

# Grab valid names straight from your enum
VALID_INSTRUMENT_NAMES = {member.name for member in Instrument}


def require_cols_in_df(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    cols: Iterable[str],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    missing = [c for c in cols if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: missing required columns: \n{as_list_str(missing)}"
        )


def require_non_nan_in_series(
    ser: pd.Series,
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    bad_mask = ser.isna()
    if pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(ser.dtype):
        arr = ser.to_numpy(dtype="float64", na_value=np.nan)
        bad_mask = bad_mask | pd.Series(np.isinf(arr), index=ser.index)

    if bad_mask.any():
        bad_idx = ser.index[bad_mask].tolist()
        raise PrettyKeyError(
            f"{ctx}: found {len(bad_idx)} invalid values at rows: {bad_idx[:10]}"
        )


def require_positive_values(
    ser: pd.Series,
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    valid_mask = ser.notna()
    violators_mask = ser[valid_mask].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64) <= 0
    if violators_mask.any():
        bad_idx = ser[valid_mask].index[violators_mask].tolist()
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: found {len(bad_idx)} non-positive values at rows: {bad_idx[:10]}"
        )


def validate_instruments(
    ser: pd.Series,
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    """Vectorized check to ensure all instruments match the defined Enum."""
    invalid_mask = ~ser.isin(VALID_INSTRUMENT_NAMES)

    if invalid_mask.any():
        bad_idx = ser.index[invalid_mask].tolist()
        bad_vals = ser[invalid_mask].unique()
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: Invalid instruments detected {bad_vals.tolist()} "
            f"at rows: {bad_idx[:10]}. Valid options are: {VALID_INSTRUMENT_NAMES}"
        )


def validate_is_call_flags(
    ser: pd.Series,
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    """Ensure is_call is strictly 0 (put), 1 (call), or missing/NaN."""
    # We only care about rows where the user actually provided a value
    valid_mask = ser.notna()

    # Check if any provided values are NOT 0 or 1
    provided_vals = ser[valid_mask]
    violators_mask = ~provided_vals.isin([0, 1, 0.0, 1.0])

    if violators_mask.any():
        bad_idx = provided_vals.index[violators_mask].tolist()
        bad_vals = provided_vals[violators_mask].unique()
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: 'is_call' must be 0, 1, or blank. Found {bad_vals.tolist()} "
            f"at rows: {bad_idx[:10]}"
        )


def validate_conditional_option_fields(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    """
    Cross-column validation: If it is an option, it MUST have a strike and expiry.
    """
    # Create a fast mask for any row that is an option
    is_option_mask = df["instrument"].str.contains("OPTION", na=False)

    if not is_option_mask.any():
        return  # No options to check

    # Check strikes
    if (
        "strike_price" not in df.columns
        or df.loc[is_option_mask, "strike_price"].isna().any()
    ):
        bad_idx = df[
            is_option_mask
            & df.get("strike_price", pd.Series(np.nan, index=df.index)).isna()
        ].index.tolist()
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: Options must have a 'strike_price'. Missing at rows: {bad_idx[:10]}"
        )

    # Check expiries
    if (
        "expiry_date" not in df.columns
        or df.loc[is_option_mask, "expiry_date"].isna().any()
    ):
        bad_idx = df[
            is_option_mask
            & df.get("expiry_date", pd.Series(np.nan, index=df.index)).isna()
        ].index.tolist()
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: Options must have an 'expiry_date'. Missing at rows: {bad_idx[:10]}"
        )


def validate_uids_unique(ser: pd.Series, *, ctx: str) -> None:
    """
    Ensure all trade UIDs are strictly unique across the ingestion batch.
    """
    if not ser.is_unique:
        # Only pay the cost of finding the exact duplicates if we fail
        duplicates = ser[ser.duplicated()].unique()
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: Trade UIDs must be strictly unique. "
            f"Found duplicates: {duplicates.tolist()[:10]}"
        )


def require_non_zero_values(
    ser: pd.Series,
    *,
    ctx: str,
    tol: float = POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE,
) -> None:
    """
    Vectorized check to ensure values are meaningfully non-zero.
    Uses the engine's global dust tolerance to filter out upstream float artifacts.
    """
    valid_mask = ser.notna()
    arr = ser[valid_mask].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)

    # Vectorized check for values that are effectively zero
    violators_mask = np.abs(arr) < tol

    if violators_mask.any():
        bad_idx = ser[valid_mask].index[violators_mask].tolist()
        preview = bad_idx[:25]
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: found {len(bad_idx):_.0f} trades with effectively zero quantity (abs(qty) < {tol}). "
            f"Zero-quantity and 'dust' trades carry no risk and must be filtered out upstream. "
            f"Row indices: \n{as_list_str(preview)}"
        )

```

### FILE: portfolio\instruments.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/instruments.py
from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class Instrument(IntEnum):
    """Enum for financial instruments in the risk quant portfolio."""

    SPOT = 0
    FORWARD_NODE = 1
    FORWARD_CURVE = 2
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE = 3
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE = 4

    @classmethod
    def all_instrument_ids(cls) -> set[int]:
        """Get a set of all valid instrument IDs."""
        return {member.value for member in cls}

    @classmethod
    def from_id(cls, id_: int) -> "Instrument":
        """Get the Instrument enum member corresponding to the given ID."""
        return cls(id_)

    @classmethod
    def num_instruments(cls) -> int:
        """Get the total number of instruments defined."""
        return len(cls)


# Get integer values for instruments for easy reference when building trade records in builders/ modules
SPOT_ID: int = Instrument.SPOT.value
FORWARD_NODE_ID: int = Instrument.FORWARD_NODE.value
FORWARD_CURVE_ID: int = Instrument.FORWARD_CURVE.value
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID: int = (
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE.value
)
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID: int = (
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE.value
)


# All valid instrument IDs for quick validation in trade record creation
ALL_INSTRUMENT_IDS: set[int] = Instrument.all_instrument_ids()


# Total number of instruments defined (for dispatch table sizing)
NUM_INSTRUMENTS: int = Instrument.num_instruments()

```

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class PnlResult:
    """
    Result container for position-level PnL simulations.

    Attributes:
        trade_uids: (M,) int64 array; row i of pnl corresponds to trade_uids[i]
        pnl:        (M, S) PnL matrix, expressed in each trade's settlement_ccy
    """

    pnl: np.ndarray  # float32/64, shape (M, S), contiguous

### FILE: simlab\apply_shocks\shock_kernel.py ###
```python
# src/riskquant/simlab/apply/shock_kernel.py
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numba as nb
from typing import Optional

from riskquant.simlab.scenarios.base import ScenarioSetBatch
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.shift_types import (
    SHIFT_DIFFERENCE,
    SHIFT_LOG_RETURN,
    SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN,
)

# ============================================================
# ZERO-ALLOCATION NUMBA APPLY KERNEL
# ============================================================


@nb.njit(parallel=True, fastmath=True, cache=True)
def _apply_shocks_numba_core(
    base_levels: np.ndarray,  # (N,) float64
    shocks: np.ndarray,  # (B, K) float64 (Fortran-contiguous)
    req_to_uix: np.ndarray,  # (N,) int32
    shock_scale: np.ndarray,  # (N,) float64
    shift_types: np.ndarray,  # (N,) int8
    out: np.ndarray,  # (N, B) float64 (C-contiguous)
):
    """
    The inner JIT-compiled loop.
    Parallelized across the N required exposures.
    """
    N = base_levels.shape[0]
    B = shocks.shape[0]

    # Parallelize over the portfolio exposures (N)
    for n in nb.prange(N):
        # 1. Fetch factor-specific metadata once per row
        base = base_levels[n]
        k = req_to_uix[n]
        scale = shock_scale[n]
        st = shift_types[n]

        # Pull the shock column once per row. This is the only way to avoid
        # redundant memory reads of the huge shocks array inside the B-loop.
        shock_col = shocks[:, k]

        # 2. Branching is hoisted OUTSIDE the B-loop.
        # The CPU executes the inner loop with zero branch prediction misses.
        if st == SHIFT_LOG_RETURN:
            for b in range(B):
                # out is (N, B) C-contiguous.
                # Looping over b writes sequentially into memory. Cache-perfect.
                out[n, b] = base * np.exp(shock_col[b] * scale)

        elif st == SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN:
            for b in range(B):
                out[n, b] = base * (1.0 + (shock_col[b] * scale))

        elif st == SHIFT_DIFFERENCE:
            for b in range(B):
                out[n, b] = base + (shock_col[b] * scale)


# ============================================================
# PYTHON DISPATCH WRAPPER
# ============================================================


def apply_shocks_to_base_levels(
    *,
    base_levels: np.ndarray,
    batch: ScenarioSetBatch,
    out: Optional[np.ndarray] = None,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Unpacks the Python ScenarioSetBatch dataclass and feeds the raw
    NumPy arrays into the Numba kernel.
    """
    N = base_levels.shape[0]
    B = batch.shocks.shape[0]

    # Force shocks to be Fortran-contiguous for optimal access patterns in the Numba kernel.
    if not batch.shocks.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
        shocks = np.asfortranarray(batch.shocks)
    else:
        shocks = batch.shocks

    # Pre-allocate if not provided. In the hot VaR loop,
    # you should always be passing a slice of the global array here.
    if out is None:
        out = np.empty((N, B), dtype=np.float64, order="C")

    _apply_shocks_numba_core(
        base_levels=base_levels,
        shocks=shocks,
        req_to_uix=batch.required_to_shock_uix,
        shock_scale=batch.shock_scale,
        shift_types=batch.required_shift_type_id,
        out=out,
    )

    return out

```
